# Changelog

All the changes of the system that a reader notices. The format is [Keep a Changelog 1.1.0](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).

The project has steps, not separate releases. The merge of step N on `master` is the release `0.N.0`. A correction between two steps is `0.N.1`, `0.N.2`. The release `0.0.0` is the first milestone, before step 1. A heading gives the release time in UTC; an old heading whose time no record holds gives its day alone. Each release from `modustool ship` has the signed release tag `v<number>`, and the links at the end of this file point to the tags. The number of the newest release is `RELEASE_NUMBER` in `src/library/contract/release/release.odin`; `modustool release_number` prints it.

What is not in a release yet is in `changelog.d/`, one fragment for each change. `modustool ship` writes a release from the fragments, commits it, tags it and pushes it. Nobody edits this file by hand.

## [0.98.0] - 2026-11-25 16:08

### Added

- **The interrupt window of a guest**: `Guest_Run` now delivers an `External_Interrupt` only when the guest takes interrupts; with the interrupt flag of the guest clear or in its interrupt shadow, the kernel opens the interrupt window, and the run ends with the new exit `Interrupt_Window` when the guest takes interrupts again, with the request still in the record. An external interrupt below the vector 32 is refused, an external interrupt in the shadow is no longer refused, and the record gives `event_pending`: 1 when the guest processor holds an event that an exit interrupted.
- **Write-combining device memory**: `Memory_Map` takes the flag `Write_Combining` for device memory of `Device_Kind.Memory`, which a device service maps write-combining in place of uncached, for a shared region such as the host-visible memory of a virtual GPU. The kernel writes write-combining in the page attribute table of each processor at its start, and each processor reads it back; the flag is refused on normal memory, on the memory of a screen and with `Execute`, the mappings of a memory object of a device, and of each object over the same pages, keep one cache type (`Bad_State` else), and the report of the mappings names such a mapping `Write_Combining`.
- **The plan of principal 0 at the start proved across a restart.** The machine test `persist` applies a plan of principal 0 that names another holder of the role Settings, accepts its difference on the trusted display, and restarts the machine: the stored plan holds before the start policy, so the row `settings` of the start list starts that holder from the first start. Before it, a stale expected plan, a difference that the principal refuses, a holder that the image lacks and a holder that does not fill the contract are each refused with no generation committed; a disk with no plan ends the wait at the answer of `plans`, never at the hang bound.
- **The audit of the collector proved on the machine.** The machine test `persist` starts the collector and a source with a list whose row pairs `collector.forward` with that source: each record comes to its reader through the pair, in its order, after the audit space kept it, and both audit spaces and their hash chains are whole after a stop of the collector and after a restart of the machine. A pair with no source row and a second pair of one source are refused.
- **A double press on a title bar maximizes the window.** Two presses on the title of a window within 400 ms and 4 pixels toggle its maximize, and a drag of the title of a maximized window gives it back its size of before under the pointer.
- **A module category and tags in each module file.** The header of a module file says what the module is to a reader (an application, a system tool, a developer tool, a service, a library, a demonstration or a test) and up to twelve words of what it is about. The build refuses a category that the folder of the source does not allow, and a reader refuses a word that names nothing. A fifth module group, `applications`, waits for its first program.
- **Alt+Tab switches windows.** Alt+Tab opens a card of the windows, the one used last first, and chooses the window used before the one in front; each Tab chooses the next, Alt+Shift+Tab goes backward, the release of Alt brings the chosen window to the front, and Escape closes the card. The input service now gives the left Alt as the modifier bit `MODIFIER_ALT` of each key; the right Alt key stays AltGr.
- **A console variable keeps a list**: `x = [1, 2, 3]` keeps the list, and `map(x, (n) => n * n)` on a later line gives `[1, 4, 9]`. A variable keeps 64 items at most, nested lists included, and 32 KiB of their texts and records; a list past a bound is refused with an error record `Over_Limit`, and the variable keeps its old value. A variable still keeps no function.
- **A call of an export connects its service**: `monitor.processes()` before `connect("monitor")` connects the service when the call runs, after its arguments, with the same check and the same log line as `connect`. Only an export that the module file of the boot image has connects; a call that the line does not force connects nothing.
- **The console suggests the names near a wrong name**: an unknown command, variable, field or help topic gets up to three names one to three edits away, as `no command has the name proceses; did you mean processes?`. A field counted in the unit that the name says comes first: `where(processes(), pages > 1000)` suggests `resident` and `mapped`.
- **`describe(name)` explains one name**: a command gives its signature and its help, and a word of Lex (`let`, `in`, `with`, `|>`, `_`, `=>`, `if`) or `map`, `filter`, `where` and `connect` give a short guide with an example. `help()` stays the table of the commands, and `help("let")` now says to use `describe("let")`.
- **The pages of the browser machine show the stages of the start**: the download with its bytes, the start of the kernel and of the root service, the image check, the start of the services, then of the desktop or of the console, each with its time, in place of a black canvas or of the word starting. `modustool browser-machine boot` prints the time of each stage.
- **A Linux shell in a window**: `linux` in the start menu boots the Linux kernel of its private store in a guest and gives its serial console in a terminal window: the lines of Linux are the lines of the terminal, each key goes to the shell of the guest, an idle guest waits for a key with no processor use, and `poweroff` in the guest ends the process. The machine test boots the bzImage of Nixpkgs with a busybox to its shell in about 20 s. The guest machine now takes input on its serial port, answers the debug registers and the one-shot mode of its timer, and runs Linux with `printk.time=0` and with no jitter entropy source; the guest builder and `linux` share the code that maps the memory of a guest (`modus:guest_regions`).
- **A clock application.** The process `clock` shows the local time in large digits, the date with its time zone, and the calendar of a month; a stopwatch with laps and a countdown timer are in two more sections, with the keys 1, 2 and 3. At the end of the timer the window shows 00:00 and the process asks the compositor for a desktop notification.
- **A maximized window shows a restore button.** Its button draws two windows, and the palette, the menu of the desktop and the bar name the act "restore" while the window is maximized; the palette has the entry "maximize window".
- **The background work of a window.** `interface.Background_Work` runs a call that waits for another process on a worker thread, one job at a time, and wakes the loop of the window at its end. `paint` keeps its pictures and `clock` posts its notification through it, so neither window stops drawing while the store or the compositor answers.
- **A function written with `->` gets its form in the error**: `map(x, n -> n * n)` fails with "`->` is no operator: a function is written (n) => n * n", at the `->`. Lex keeps one form of a function, `(n) => n * n`, and no alias.
- **A long wait of the TPM says what it waits for.** When the TPM service or the unlock window waits for the TPM, a wait report on the log each 30 seconds names the step, the TPM command and the request that waits, and a last line names the bound that ended the wait. A slow TPM still has its long bounds.
- **The assistant: an agent from the desktop**: the window `assistant` takes a request of the principal and shows the reply of an agent, each act that it proposes and its result; a result that is a table or a record shows as the console shows it, with aligned columns, each number at the right with its unit in the head, one line for each row, a line that names the columns that do not fit, and the count of the rows that are not shown. The agent runner `modustool assistant` on the host reads the request through `remote`, asks a language model whose endpoint and key stay on the host, refuses a malformed model reply whole with its error record, and runs each act only through the host agent bridge. The endpoint is this machine unless `--key-in-clear-off-host` accepts that the key crosses the network in clear; an answer of the model has 120 seconds; the model reads the last 16 requests, and the outcome of an act speaks as a tool, never as the principal. An act that the trusted display cannot show takes a final state and holds back no later act.
- **The act dialog of the trusted display**: an act of an agent that the bridge stops shows "TRUSTED ACT" with the asker, read from the connection, and the act as `service.export`, never a word of the agent. The focus starts on Refuse, so Enter refuses; Tab moves the focus only after the arm time and starts it again, so Tab then Enter at once never allows. The decision goes to the asker only, and the dialog closes with a refusal when the asker exits or withdraws the act. `compositor.act_ask`, `compositor.act_poll` and `compositor.act_cancel` serve a process with the right to ask an act, which opens no other export of the trusted display.
- **A window that scrolls when it does not fit the work area.** Each window gets at most the work area, at each scale and from its first picture, and keeps its layout: a window whose layout is larger scrolls it, with a scroll bar on each side that does not fit, the wheel, Page Up and Page Down, and the view moving to the control that Tab reaches. A window that the work area cut gets its size back when the work area grows. Settings opened at 200 % fits the screen, and Shift+Tab reaches its last control.
- **The first program described as a Lex project.** The folder src/outside/projects/collatz holds a small C program with its project.lex and pins.lex, the form that each next program of the system copies; the evaluator reads it, the service projects loads it, and its output names the recipe of its module.
- **The page of a public object cache.** `modustool cache-serve --status-listen <host:port>` answers a page in the look of the site and `status.json`: what modus and this object cache are, that a client trusts a hash or a signer key and never the host, the accepted signer keys, the endpoint and its protocol (binary frames over TCP), the counts of the store, the free space and the last 10 realisations. The server keeps its counts at each write, so a view of the page reads no file, and its reader refuses a head past 4096 bytes, a head that does not end in 5 seconds, a method other than `GET` and any other path.
- **The screen through the scanout of the virtual GPU.** The machine of `modustool run` has a VirtIO-GPU with its VGA part, and the compositor shows each frame through the scanout of the GPU: one batch of the damage rectangles of the frame, so the display of the host redraws only what changed, and each frame stays whole. While the pointer moved at 125 moves each second (KVM, 1920 x 1080, the GTK display in Xvfb), QEMU redrew about 1.07 million pixels each second, against 67 to 89 million with the page flip of the standard VGA, at the same 62 frames each second. At rest it redraws the clock only: 3800 pixels each second. The new machine test part `gpu_scanout` checks that a picture of QEMU is the frame of the compositor.
- **A program written, built and run inside the system**: `projects.new("hello")` makes a project, `edit("hello", "hello.c")` takes its C source, `build("hello", "hello.c")` compiles it with the native C builder of `recipes` into a module in the store `module_files`, and `run("hello")` calls its export `main` in a call host with no grant. The same source again is substituted with no compile, a C error names its byte and builds nothing, and a grant that the project does not declare is refused.
- **Each release on the public object cache.** A release puts each file of its boot image, by its BLAKE3 hash, and one realisation signed with a new cache signing key on `cache.modus.systems:5010`, then reads them back; `modustool cache-put` and `cache-get` put and take objects and realisations on any object cache, and `cache-get` refuses a byte that does not have its hash and a realisation that the trusted signer did not sign.
- **The catalog of the system written by its build.** Each build now writes `build/catalog.lex` for the boot image and `build/catalog_release.lex` for the release boot image without test modules, giving `catalog.release`, `catalog.modules.<name>`, and `catalog.c.module(path)` from the exact BLAKE3 hashes of the built modules so projects evaluate without hand-written hashes.
- **A builder started in its sandbox.** `recipes.build` now runs a builder module named by its hash in the recipe. The service `recipes` starts it as a call host with a read-only input store that holds only the objects of its inputs, an empty store "out", no network and no root ring, and a seed equal to the recipe hash. The output hash is the tree hash of the store "out". A builder that writes its inputs, fails, stops, or runs past the bound of 60 s is refused, and nothing is kept. A builder that is no module of the boot image is refused with `Builder_Absent`, and an input that no build gave with `Input_Absent`. A share of the storage service now gives its handles with the right Give, so its maker can pass them to another process, and a share refuses to make a share.
- **A check of the display texts of the modules.** `modustool check` and `modustool rule-checks` now run `display_text_check`, which inspects module `member_texts`, setting descriptions and labels, window problem sentences, action bar items, buttons, and context menu items and refuses forbidden words from `docs/WRITING.md`, contractions, "Error" or "Failure" titles, "failed" alone, wrong sentence case, and wrong end periods.
- **No line cut by a reading tool**: `modustool check` refuses each file that holds the mark that a reading tool leaves at the end of a cut line, and the intent files of the resize of windows and of the recipes have their whole lines back.
- **Several steps of an agent for one request**: the assistant answers a request in up to 16 steps of up to 16 acts each; it finds the commands of the system itself with `manual.modules`, `manual.exports` and `manual.signature`, reads each typed result, and goes on until its answer. It stops at 16 steps, at a step that repeats the acts and the results of the step before, or when you press Stop or Escape, and the window shows each step once, folds the results of older steps, and says once why the work ended. A request now holds 4096 bytes, a reply 16 KiB, and a table result carries 64 rows that the window pages through.
- **The authority key of the wide grants given at the start.** The root service makes a fresh authority key at each start from a seed of the strength `Processor` or stronger, and gives it to the collector in its context, so the collector checks the proof of a wide grant in place of refusing each one; a weak seed gives no key, and the collector then refuses each wide grant.
- **The act records of a wide grant kept by the collector from the grant on.** At each wide grant the collector opens one audit space for the acts of the root service, the agent console, `remote` and the trusted display, refuses the grant when its store cannot hold them, refuses each act record of another writer, grant or principal, and writes the end of the grant at its revocation or its expiry; the trusted display drops the input of a viewer whose act record finds its ring full.
- **The conversation of the assistant scrolls**: the newest entry shows until you move up with the wheel, the scroll bar, Page Up or Page Down, or Control with Home or End, and the view then keeps its place while the agent goes on. A reply or a result longer than the view shows the lines that fit and a button that shows the others.
- **The cache browser in `modustool cache-serve`, recipe objects, and signed release pointers.** The status listener of `cache-serve` now serves read-only HTML pages in the style of the site for trees (`/tree/<hash>`, paged by 100 entries), blobs (`/blob/<hash>` with module metadata, text, PNG, or hexadecimal preview, and sandboxed raw download at `/blob/<hash>/raw`), realisations (`/realisation/<recipe hash>` with signature checks and recipe inputs when the recipe object is held), release pointers (`/releases`), and bounded prefix search (`/search?h=<prefix>`), checking every read object against its hash before showing any byte. The cache wire protocol adds `Put_Release_Pointer` and `Get_Release_Pointer`, `plans_recipes` adds canonical recipe object encoding, and `cache-put release` publishes the recipe object, the release trees, and a signed release pointer.
- **The guest builder.** `recipes.build` of a recipe whose builder is the guest builder packs its inputs into a read-only disk, runs the command of its spec in a Linux guest with a blank output disk, no network and a frozen time, and keeps the tree of the output disk as the realisation of the recipe. A build is refused, with nothing kept, when the guest exits with a status that is not 0, writes a damaged or oversize output disk, writes to its input disk or reaches the bound of its exits. The new export `recipes.put` keeps a blob or a tree by its hash, so a recipe names it as an input.
- **Objects of any size in an object cache.** `cache-serve`, `cache-put` and `cache-get` carry a blob of any size, such as a release disk or the kernel of a guest, in frames of 1 MiB, and each side checks each frame against the BLAKE3 hash of the whole blob, so a blob keeps its one name. The bound of 16 MiB on one object is gone: each side holds a few frames in memory, whatever the size of the object, and `cache-get` of a blob to a file resumes after an interruption. The protocol is now version 2, and a server or a client of version 1 is refused.
- **A remote display of a 4K, 5K or 8K screen.** A module states the pictures of the size of the screen that it holds, and the root service pays them at the size of the screen of the machine, so `remote_display` and the compositor follow a 4K, 5K or 8K screen as far as the share of the machine for a process holds. When the service cannot hold the screen in force, each viewer ends with the code `Screen_Not_Held` and one line counts them.
- **Combining marks, Arabic, Hebrew, the ideographs and emoji**: a letter and its combining marks draw the composed letter (e and U+0301 draw é, Vietnamese stacked marks draw ệ), a mark that does not compose draws over its letter, and a mark with no letter stands on a dotted circle. Arabic letters join in their initial, medial and final forms with the lam-alef ligature, and a line that mixes left-to-right and right-to-left text keeps each run in its order, with mirrored brackets. The fonts of Hebrew, Arabic, the geometric shapes, the emoji and the CJK ideographs are one script font set of 35.8 MB outside the boot image, checked by its hash; a process with no set still draws the box for those scripts, and the plan field that names the set comes next.
- **A screen of memory for a machine with no screen.** A machine with no display device, such as a server, runs the desktop and the console in memory, at 1920 × 1080 by default, and a viewer of `remote_display` sees it and drives it. A viewer with the input can ask another size, at most once each second: the desktop takes it and the windows fit it again, or the viewer gets the cause of the refusal and keeps its session. The budgets pay the screen at the size of its start, and grow for a larger size. The remote display protocol is now version 3, and the start list is now format version 4, with the size of the screen of memory on its row `display`.
- **The rings of the act records wired by the root service at the grant.** A wide grant now holds only once its act records have a reader: the root service sends the grant to the collector of the principal, then wires the record outputs `acts` of `remote` and of the trusted display to it, and ends these wirings with the grant; `remote` writes a record before each call of a key under a grant, and refuses the call with `Busy` when the ring is full.
- **A machine test of a guest build.** The test part `guest_build` builds a recipe of the guest builder on a machine: a static `init` in the guest unpacks the input disk, runs the command with busybox, writes the output disk and gives its exit status, and the realisation names the tree hash that the host computed. The new export `recipes.build_stored` builds a recipe of the store named by its hash as text.
- **The screen, the proof and the mark of a wide grant.** `agent_allow(key, scopes, minutes)` shows the screen of a wide grant on the trusted display, where the principal picks the scopes, the windows and the duration, then types the passphrase in `unlock`; the bar shows the mark with the minutes left and revoke, and the keys and the pointer of a viewer reach only the windows picked. The grant ends at `agent_disallow`, at revoke, at its end time and at the lock.
- **A removal of an object larger than 1 GiB.** The storage service frees the blocks of a state too large for one commit in steps, through the removal queue of its store, so a principal removes a video of several GiB in one call; a loss of power loses no step.
- **A folder of more than 4096 entries in one content tree.** A frozen folder of more entries is a tree of several nodes of 4096 entries under nodes of 64 parts, so a program freezes a folder of any size; a folder of at most 4096 entries keeps its one node and its hash.
- **A data disk larger than the memory of the storage service.** The counts of the names of the blocks live on the disk in a count tree, which each commit writes with copy on write, so a disk of several terabytes opens with a small budget, from its count tree, with no walk of its blocks; when the memory holds a table of each block, the start still walks and checks each block and compares the tree with it. The format of the disk is `STORAGE6`: a disk of an earlier format is not read.
- **A screen of memory that grows.** A viewer asks a screen of memory larger than its start size, and the root service gives the compositor and `remote_display` the pages of that size at the same step, within the share of a process, or refuses both with the cause. A screen that grew grows again: for a moment each process holds the pages of the old size and of the new one, when its share pays both, then the pages of the old size return. The pages return when the size comes back to at most the start size, when the last viewer leaves, as the screen goes back to its start size, or when either process stops. The root service counts the pages of an extra budget until no object of it is left, and makes at most one growth each second.
- **Devanagari**: a process with the script font set draws Devanagari as it is written, with Noto Sans Devanagari, the seventh font of the set (36 433 492 bytes): the half forms and the conjuncts, the reph over its base, the vowel sign ि before its consonant, and the marks placed by the font. The glyphs of 34 words are those of HarfBuzz on the same font. A syllable is never cut by a fit, and a face of cells does not shape it.
- **The grants of a role follow its holder.** A holder of a role that the plan of principal 0 names gets the grants of the default holder of that role, and the store of the role in place of a store of its module name, so a replaced holder does real work and finds the values of the role.
- **The display settings survive a restart.** The edge of the bar, do not disturb, the show time of a notification, the factor of the magnifier and the consent arm time are kept by the compositor in the store of the role Display, so a restarted compositor or a replaced holder gets them back. `set`, the getters, `reset` and `settings_state` of the Display role read and change them.
- **A kernel operation reads a memory object by its handle.** `Memory_Read` (36) copies up to 1 MiB of an object that the caller holds with Read into its own memory, and gives a status where a read through a mapping would fault: `Bad_State` for a page that is not committed or that `Memory_Protect` marked No_Access, `Bad_Address` for a destination in a mapping of the same object. The kernel checks each page before the first byte, so a refused read writes nothing; `system.memory_read` copies a larger range in several calls. `Memory_Size` now also gives the flags of the creation that the object keeps (`Contiguous`, `Uncommitted`, `Large_Pages`), which `system.memory_size_and_flags` returns.
- **The shaping of the other scripts of India**: a process with the script font set draws Bengali, Gurmukhi, Gujarati, Oriya, Tamil, Telugu, Kannada and Malayalam as they are written, each with its Noto font, on the shaper of Devanagari: the conjuncts, the below and post forms, the reph at the place of its script, the vowel signs of two parts, the pre-base forms of ra. Each glyph, advance and offset of 142 test words is that of HarfBuzz on the same fonts. The script font set holds 15 fonts, 40 030 968 bytes.
- **The script fonts named in the plan and mapped by the root service**: Hebrew, Arabic, the ideographs, the emoji and the geometric shapes now draw on a machine. The boot image names the script font set by its hash, and the plan of principal 0 can name another one; when the machine is usable, the root service reads the set from the store `script_fonts` of the data disk, checks its hash, and maps it into each process that starts after that. A machine with no set, or with a set whose hash does not match, keeps the replacement box for those scripts.
- **The ask of an agent, and its connection ended at the end of its grant.** An agent asks for a wide grant through `remote`, with `ask_wide_grant` of the agent bridge, for the key of its own connection only; the trusted display shows the same screen as for `agent_allow`. At the end of the grant the connection of the key ends with `Revoked`, and a second connection of the key while the grant holds ends the grant and both connections.
- **The audit of a server from the start list.** A wiring row of a start list carries the terms of an audit space, so the collector keeps each record that leaves a server, and its log, from the start of the machine. The records that enter a server stay out: their writer would be a process that the start list did not start.
- **The machine cases of the script font set that wait for a module of their own.** The machine test applies a plan of principal 0 that names another script font set and maps it at the next start, checks that a write to the mapped set stops the process that writes on a page fault at its first byte, and checks that a set with the right hash and a refused form maps nothing.
- **A repair of the count tree that pauses the changes.** When a leaf or a directory of the count tree of a large disk has a wrong check, the storage service serves the reads and answers each change with `Busy` (-14, unit `Count_Repair`, with the stores walked and the stores in all) while it counts the names of its blocks again, then writes the leaves in one commit and takes changes again; the client of the storage service makes such a change again for up to 60 seconds, so a program sees it pass after the repair. A loss of power during the repair starts it again at the next start.
- **Marks placed by their font, Thai, and the isolates of a line**: a combining mark sits where its font puts it, on its letter or on the mark below it, in place of a centre; the letters of Arabic take the forms of their font with their dots, so the extended letters join too and a vowel sign sits above the dots. Thai draws with Noto Sans Thai, its tone marks stacked and SARA AM split as Thai readers expect. A line that holds an isolate of UAX #9 (LRI, RLI, FSI to PDI) keeps it as one unit with its own direction, and the controls of direction, the joiners and the spaces of no width draw nothing in place of a box.
- **Plan defaults and plan-only values.** A plan of principal 0 gives values to the owners of settings: a default under the choice of the principal, or a lock over it, for the settings of `settings` and of the display, and the values that only the plan holds: the retention rule of the versions, the quota of a store, the share of the machine for one process and the sizes of the screen of memory. Each owner reads them at its next start; the retention rule and the quota of an open store hold from the apply, and after each start of the machine. Each change of one asks the consent of the principal on the trusted display.
- **A DHCP client with a static address from the plan.** The network service takes the address of the machine from DHCP, with its lease and its renewal, and refuses each reply whose fields a machine cannot hold; a plan of principal 0 can give a static address in its place. Until an address holds, a request of an endpoint waits for it.
- **Module settings, with the browser.** A module that holds no role has settings in the same form as `settings` and the compositor, kept in its private store: `browser.set(home = "http://example.org/")` chooses the address that the browser opens at its start, 1 to 160 bytes that its address field takes, and `monitor.set(sample_interval = 1000)` the time between two pictures of the monitor, 250 ms to 5000 ms. Each has a getter, `reset` and `settings_state`, and a plan can give the default of the monitor or lock it.
- **The lock of a value.** A plan of principal 0 can lock a setting: its value holds over the choice of the principal, and each setter and `reset` refuses a change of it with `No_Grant`, unit `Plan_Lock`: "the argument font is locked by the plan of principal 0". A press in the window of settings, on the desktop or on the tile of the appearance changes nothing under a lock, and the window writes "locked by the plan" after the name of the setting. The choice of the principal stays kept, and holds again when the lock ends; `settings_state` gives the layer `Locked`.
- **A setting can be a text.** An optional parameter of an export can be a Text, with the bound of its bytes in its description (`parameter_ranges = "home = 1..160"`): the console refuses a text past the bound before the call, and the owner keeps the text in its record of chosen values, which refuses a text that is not UTF-8.
- **A plan written as `plan { ... }` reaches `plans.apply`.** `plans.apply("home", plan { values = { browser = { home = "modus://guide" } }, locks = { settings = { appearance = .dark } } }, {})` applies a plan written in the console: roles by the hash of their module, store grants, defaults and locks. Each value is typed by the description of its owner before the call, and a refusal names what is wrong: the owner, the field, the member, the range or the bound.
- **A plan gives a text value.** A plan of principal 0 gives the default of a text setting, as the home address of the browser, or locks it. The trusted display shows the text whole, in quotes, over as many lines as it takes, and its lists no longer split an item at a comma.
- **The peak of a budget, checked at the first state of work.** A budget keeps the least count of its free pages since its creation, which the report of a process gives; a process says its first state of work on its root ring (`system.ready`, the command `Ready`), and the root service writes its peak with its floor, and a second line when the peak passed the floor, which fails each machine test. The console says it today.
- **A closed audit space ends after its retention.** An audit space that closed when its module changed the size of its record is kept 90 days from its close, then removed, and its place in the collector is free. A reader of a removed space gets the refusal `Space_Removed`, with the kind `Absent`, not a part of the audit; a removal that stops goes on at the next pass, also after a restart.
- **Khmer, Myanmar and the scripts of the Universal Shaping Engine.** A process with the script font set draws Khmer, Myanmar, Tibetan, Balinese, Javanese and Sundanese with their stacked letters, their vowel signs before the consonant and their marks in place, where it drew the replacement glyph. Each glyph, advance and offset of 154 test words of the six scripts is the one that HarfBuzz 12.3.0 gives on the same fonts. The script font set now holds 21 fonts.
- **Every process says its first state of work, and the floors are measured.** Each process that the root service starts gets a ready ring at the handle 19 of its context, on which it says once that its start is done: the library says it at the first present of a window and at the first wait of a service, and the services and drivers before their main loop, so the machine test checks the floor of each of them. `modustool floors measured <log>` reads the peaks of a machine run and prints, for each module, the working memory that makes its floor its peak.
- **A measure for the modules that run once and stop.** A process that never said its first state of work says it in `system.stop` and waits for the root service to read the peak of its budget, at most the answer bound of a ready ring, so the floor of each test module that computes and stops is checked and measured too; the two serial terminals say it before their loop.
- **The blocks of the stores of a principal encrypted.** Each store of a named principal now encrypts its data blocks and secret metadata with vector XChaCha20, authenticates every metadata and store block with a 128-bit keyed BLAKE3 SIV tag, verifies data blocks against their authenticated parent node values or entry hash and check, rejects older store block generations within a session, and erases the principal's keys, cached blocks, hash-index entries, pending pools, and transient buffers at lock.
- **Typed SQLite module and db_browser application**: the SQLite module now exposes typed records, per-call database byte handles, savepoint rollback on multi-statement errors, and dirty-range tracking; the new `db_browser` project provides a two-thread graphical SQLite browser with a SQL editor, virtualized sortable result grid, table and column sidebar, and copy-on-write version history.
- **The palette and the window of settings read the range and the choices.** The palette now supports up to 12 parameters per command, chunked text arguments up to 1024 bytes, and setting rows and form candidates from module parameter ranges and choices exports. The window of settings discovers module settings from the boot image and renders cards for them alongside the core sections.
- **Store Explorer application and JavaScript runtime study**: added the outside `store_explorer` graphical application to browse stores and nested trees, filter entries, inspect UTF-8 text or binary objects with their BLAKE3 hash, create or remove entries, and preview, compare, or restore copy-on-write versions; added `docs/research/js_runtime_study.md` evaluating Bun and QuickJS-NG against the properties of modus.
- **A C builder.** The module c_builder exports build of the builder contract, compiles a C file of its input tree with c_compiler and assemble in zero-grant child call hosts, and catalog.c.module targets it.
- **A source tree imported into the objects of recipes.** recipes unifies its private store under 64-hex BLAKE3 object names, adds put_memory and read_output with per-principal quotas and the 75% retention guard, and the console adds recipes.ingest_tree, recipes.build_project, recipes.provenance, and recipes.deliver_module.
- **The cache protocol over QUIC.** modustool cache-serve serves the v2 Bao-framed object cache protocol with Get_References over QUIC on UDP port 5010 alongside TCP, and recipes fetches verified realisations and multi-frame Bao objects over QUIC with a pinned server key.
- **Lex v2 engine, language ergonomics, and incremental evaluation cache.** Lex now compiles through a 16-byte SoA AST with native `|>` parsing into a flat 32-bit register bytecode VM with 16-byte values, 24-byte union thunks, O(1) tail-call optimization, UTF-8 sorted record shapes with per-shape hash indices, unbounded budget-governed declaration bundles, string escapes and `${...}` interpolation, `&&`/`||`/`!`/`++`, strict record destructuring, pure built-in functions (`fold`, `zip`, `fields`, `values`, `has`, `extend`, `merge_deep`, `assert`, `trace`), byte-span/force-stack/nearest-name diagnostics, and a caller-owned two-layer incremental evaluation cache.
- **The conversation of the assistant kept.** The assistant stores its conversation in its private store off the interface thread in a BLAKE3-verified binary format, restores it on start, and recovers cleanly with an empty conversation if stored bytes are damaged.
- **Agent steps of Lex lines with names, in a read-only agent console.** An agent sends up to 16 lines of Lex per step with persistent per-request variable bindings, pipe filtering, 64-call and 60-second bounds, fail-closed BLAKE3 act records, and a read-only call border that refuses unauthorized calls with No_Grant and zero dialog.
- **An allowed act of the assistant runs.** A non-sensitive act allowed once by the principal on the trusted display runs once under an active wide grant through a verified single-use binding with a fail-closed BLAKE3 act record, while sensitive actions requiring fresh principal proof remain refused.
- **More acts for the assistant.** The model of the assistant proposes multi-line Lex steps, structured log fact reads, screen snapshots, and wide-grant-scoped key and pointer input through the agent bridge.
- **Add `modustool film tutorial` and allow disk processes one isolated child.** `modustool film tutorial` records an end-to-end developer tutorial covering Lex pipes, project ingestion, native C builds, signed QUIC object cache substitution, module delivery, sandboxed `db_browser` launch with an isolated `sqlite.module` child call host, live SQL commits, column sorting, and copy-on-write version previews. Disk processes can now request up to 2 process slots (paid from an 8-slot shared disk-process budget) and receive a 4096-block private store so sandboxed applications can isolate untrusted libraries cleanly.
- **The budgets set by the rows of the plan.** The plan of principal 0 now carries `default_growth_factor`, `budget_growth_period_ms`, the disk processes class budget fields on `root`, and per-owner budget policy rows (`budget_pages` or `budget_share_parts`, `budget_ceiling_pages`, `budget_growth_reserve_pages`, `budget_processor`, `budget_log_records`, `budget_screen_pages`), with a log fact when a row sits below a start floor.
- **A growth past the size as a second budget.** Processes with a plan growth ceiling can request a second budget handle through `Root_Command.Budget_Grow` (`Grow` and `Release`), rate-limited per process with an optional per-process growth reserve, drained on release and closed on stop, and refused without revealing free page counts.
- **The frames of a process.** `Memory_Commit` accepts `Commit_Flag.Frames_Now` (refused with `Decommit`), `map_region` installs present 4 KiB, 2 MiB, and 1 GiB entries while skipping empty regions, and aligned prefault commits take 2 MiB hardware runs with no privilege under a per-budget run quota, zero-allocation demotion via parked page tables, counted fallbacks in `System_Report`, and per-size entry counts in `Process_Mapping` (with 1 GiB allocator, `pdpe1gb`, and `word_table` infrastructure ready for bounded resumable zeroing).
- **Principal self-removal, reserved session budgets, and per-principal client keys.** `principal_remove()` durably destroys a named principal's key material (cryptographic erasure) and transitions its format-4 TPM pin to free while retiring the slot until reboot, each unlocked session reserves a child kernel budget and enforced storage block quota, and `remote` client keys are keyed by `(principal_identifier, public_key)` without cross-principal contests.
- **Fast iteration build mode and single-volume building.** `modustool build --fast [volume]` and `modustool test --fast <part>` compile user modules without full optimization (`Optimization_Mode.None`) and build only the requested boot volumes while keeping `-o:speed` for kernel modules, the boot program, checks, measures, and releases.
- **A limit of blocks for each share.** `storage.share` accepts a per-share block quota for writable shares that caps the share's own tree blocks and pending range buffers without thinning versions of the parent store, and `recipes` sets this quota on every builder's `"out"` share before starting the builder.
- **The collection of the objects of `recipes`.** `recipes` runs mark-and-sweep collection over its private store after local builds, before evicting live realisations when space runs low, and on demand through `recipes.collect_garbage`, removing unreferenced content-addressed objects while preserving live realisations, recipes, active or queued builds, pins, and recent puts.
- **Several frames of an object in flight.** The QUIC object cache client and `cache-serve` keep up to four 1 MiB object frame requests in flight on concurrent bidirectional QUIC streams, verifying every frame's Bao inclusion proof regardless of arrival order and committing atomically only once every frame verifies.
- **Desktop Grants window.** Added the `grants` desktop application to inspect and revoke active process grants, any active wide grant, and remembered disk grants for the current principal session with inline confirmation and off-thread root-ring queries under the least-privilege `Grants_Manage` start grant.
- **Reclaiming orphaned encrypted store blocks after principal removal.** After deliberate principal removal erases a principal's keys and deletes its principal file, the storage service scrubs the removed principal's store cells and frees all unreachable encrypted blocks and store table cells in bounded, crash-resumable commits so repeated principal creation and removal leaks no disk capacity.
- **A machine test of the exact release boot image.** The Secure Boot test volume carries the exact release boot image with no test module, and the `release_image` machine test part verifies it and boots it under Secure Boot to a usable machine.
- **The console image on QEMU.** `modustool run --console` builds the desktop-free console boot volume with `serial_terminal` on COM2 and starts the console directly in the terminal with the kernel log from COM1 written to a file and no window.
- **Lex v3 language features.** Both console and declaration modes now support nested path overrides in `with`, record field punning, typed unit literals (`bytes`, `KiB`, `MiB`, `pages`, `blocks`, `ns`, `us`, `ms`, `s`) with exact unit algebra and border conversion, `from_entries` and `{ for ... }` record comprehensions, pattern matching (`match`), safe field fallback (`??`), fifteen pure `List` and `Text` functions (`len`, `at`, `slice`, `any`, `all`, `flat_map`, `unique`, `sort_by`, `split`, `join_text`, `starts_with`, `ends_with`, `contains`, `replace_text`, `from_entries`), and optional parameter kind annotations and defaults.
- **Shared host module build cache, `--no-shots` test mode, and per-volume `--fast` module filtering.** Developers can opt into `MODUS_BUILD_CACHE=<dir>` or `--cache <dir>` to reuse verified module artifacts across worktrees and branch switches, pass `--no-shots` to `modustool test` to skip QMP screenshot captures during iteration runs, and compile only the modules and boot image needed by a single named volume under `modustool build --fast <volume>` or `modustool test --fast <part>`. Default builds, `check`, `repeat`, `screenshots`, `release`, and `ship` keep full optimization, full module builds, clean reproducibility checks, and full screenshot verification.
- **Hardware NVENC encoding and fast mode for `modustool film`.** `modustool film` now probes NVIDIA NVENC (`h264_nvenc` and `av1_nvenc`) once with automatic CPU fallback (`libx264` and `libsvtav1`), and accepts `--fast` before or after the film name to build user modules in fast iteration mode.
- **Modus Practical Course guide and five-lesson explained course film.** Added `docs/COURSE.md` (published on the documentation site), `modustool film course` (`course.mp4` and `docs/films/course.webm`) with two-panel syntax-highlighted teaching slides and rich lower-third captions covering typed tables, units, closed enum choices, bound module handles (`s = isolate("sample")`, `help("s.readings")`, `unload(s)`) and execution modes, declarative `plan { ... }` execution with trusted display consent, live `grants`, and copy-on-write versions, plus `{}` support for `Plan_Expected` in console `plans.apply` calls.
- **Console ergonomics: unified `start`, one-step `projects.install`, and `help(name)`.** `start(name)` now launches boot-image modules or pinned disk processes from `module_files` (with optional wiring `Table` or 64-digit hash `Text` as the second argument); `projects.install(name, [mode])` connects `recipes`, ingests, builds, verifies, and delivers a project module in one command returning a `Project_Install` record; and `help(name)` delegates directly to `describe(name)` including boot-image service exports and plain-English command guides.
- **Storage extent coalescing and group commit.** The storage service sorts commit writes by disk block number, coalesces contiguous block runs into multi-block transfers up to the block service limit, and groups concurrent commits to distinct stores queued in the same service pass into one flushed and read-back root commit.

### Changed

- **A terminal window grows to the whole screen.** A terminal has at most 192 columns and 54 rows, up from 104 and 32: a maximized terminal on a 1920 x 1080 screen fills its window.
- **The window of settings in sections**: Display, Pointer, Keyboard, and Region and language, each setting with a line that says what it does and the value in force marked. The scale is a track of stops from 125 % to 200 % whose stops past 137.5 % are dim; the time zone is a list of the 38 offsets in use with places; the languages are a list of names with Up and Remove. The window resizes from 400 x 320 to 1024 x 800 and reflows in one or two columns with a scroll bar. The sample card of invented numbers is gone.
- **`modustool run` shows the log as sentences.** The tool reads the serial port of the machine line by line and writes each log fact as its sentence as soon as its line ends, with the render of the machine tests; a line of text and a prompt with no end of line come as they are.
- **The start menu lists what a principal uses.** Its lines come in sections by the module category: the applications, then the tools, then the demonstrations, closed until the quick action "demonstrations" opens them; a test module, a service and a library have no line, nor a module that only another process starts, or that opens no window and writes its result on the log only. Each section is in the order of the alphabet, and a line reads "hall of fame" for `hall_of_fame`.
- **A release ships no test module.** The release volume holds the boot image of the build without the modules of the group `tests`, each other module with the bytes that the tests ran, and the release refuses a volume that holds a test module.
- **The manual groups its list and filters it.** The list of the modules is in groups by their category, each in the order of the alphabet, with the tests closed until a click opens them and a head that names the column of the export counts. Typing filters the list by name, group or tag; Escape empties the filter, and Control+O now shows the table of all the modules.
- **The demonstrations say what they show in one line.** The line that the manual and `manual.about` give for a demonstration now reads as a sentence for a reader, with the pointer to its guide after it.
- **`notes` is a notes application.** It keeps a list of notes, each named by its first line, with Ctrl+N for a new note, Ctrl+D to delete one after a question, and a press or Ctrl+Page Up and Ctrl+Page Down to show another. The text has the keys of the editor, with Enter, the selection, the clipboard and a wrap by word at the width of the resizable window. A change is one commit when the keys pause for one second or another note is shown, so a sentence is one version and not one for each key; Ctrl+H lists the versions of a note by date, and Enter restores one. The proof of one commit for each key, with its count of 1000 commits, is the test module `persistence_check`, which only the machine tests start.
- **A press on a plate of the bar acts on its window.** A press on the plate of the window in front minimizes it, and the keys go to the window behind it; a press on another plate brings its window to the front, or restores it. The right button on a plate opens a menu: bring to front or restore, minimize, close. The preview card shows only for a plate of several windows.
- **`let x = value` keeps a value at the console**: with no `in` after the value, the line is the assignment `x = value`. Before, it failed with "`,` or `in` is missing after a binding of let".
- **The monitor names its column of pages `resident`**: the window shows the field `resident` of the record `Process` of `monitor.processes()`, and its title is now that field name, not `pages`.
- **The PS/2 mouse reports 200 times a second**: the input service sets the sample rate after the probe of the wheel, which left the mouse at 80 reports a second. The compositor writes the rate of its frames while the pointer moves (`compositor: measure: the pointer moved in ... frames`).
- **The demonstration of the time page is `time_page`.** The checks and the measures of the reads of the time, which were the process `clock`, are the process `time_page`, and `modustool measure time_page` runs them.
- **A paint program.** `paint` has six tools (brush, eraser, line, rectangle, ellipse, fill), eight sizes, a palette of 16 colors and a custom color by channels, undo and redo with Ctrl+Z and Ctrl+Shift+Z, a status line, and named pictures that its private store keeps as they change: no save action, each pause of a second is one version, a change waits at most 5 seconds for its keep, each version is one whole picture, and a new start opens the picture of the newest change. Its window is resizable, and a resize never cuts the picture. The exports `line` and `write` take the 16 colors of the palette, 0 to 15.
- **The input service starts again after a stop.** The start policy starts it again at most 3 times in each minute, and the root service resets and arms the tablet again; with no PS/2 controller and a tablet, the input service goes on with the tablet, and with neither it stops with a refusal, which starts no restart.
- **The scales from 1.5 to 2.** The principal sets each scale from 1 to 2 by eighths: the track of the window of settings has nine stops, 100 % to 200 %, each selectable, and settings.set takes each member. The budget of a window counts its pictures at 2 (a window of 1000 x 704 logical pixels holds 5500 pages in place of 2600 at 1.375). A window whose budget still cannot pay a scale declines it and shows its frame only, in place of a stop of its process, and the window of settings says how many did; it offers a surface again only at another scale, and writes one line for each scale that it declines. Before a new scale, each window is fitted into the new work area: a maximized window stays maximized, a larger window gets the size that fits, and each window stays on the screen, so a terminal maximized at 1.25 shows its surface at 2. A maximize and a snap take the work area of the scale in force, not the one of the scale at the start.
- **The tile Appearance of the Control Center sets the next appearance**: a press goes auto, dark, light. With no window of settings open, the root service starts settings for one pass that keeps and writes the next appearance; with the window open, the press raises it with the focus on the appearance in force. Only the window that the root service started as settings takes the press; a second start of settings raises the window that runs, so two windows of settings never write the settings at once; and while a pass of settings runs, the press is refused and a start of the window waits until the pass ends.
- **Any connected process can post a notification.** `compositor.notify` takes a caller that does not act for the principal: its card shows the process name of the connection before the title, and the caller holds at most 3 cards at once; one more is refused with `Full`. A caller that acts for the principal posts as before.
- **Guest memory that switches page by page.** Each page of a guest mapping made with `Write`, `Execute` and `No_Access` is writable or executable, never both, and the kernel switches the one page that a nested page fault asks for, with no exit to the process, which no longer switches the mapping itself. An instruction that writes the page that holds it ends the run with the new exit `Writes_Own_Page` and the address that it writes, and the machine of a Linux guest does that one write. The kernel writes the count of switches of a guest in the log when the guest ends.
- **The decoded blocks of the software processor.** A browser machine runs each run of instructions that it decoded before from its decoded form: no translation and no decode of the prefixes for each byte, after a compare of its bytes with the memory, so code that a write changed never runs stale. In Node, with the same boot image, the processor time to the prompt of the console image fell from 4.6 s to 3.4 s, and to the first frame of the desktop image from 10.5 to 11.5 s to 8.6 s. `modustool browser-machine boot` now waits for the last line of the answer of `help()`, which the console writes whatever the count of its commands, and checks that the root service of a page refuses a module with one changed byte at its first use.
- **The budget of a resizable window counts no more than the screen**: a module now states the least size of a resizable window in its budget, as the constants that it passes to `display.resizable_set`, and the build refuses a budget whose least size differs from that call. When that least size fits 960 x 540, the logical screen at the scale 2, the tool counts each picture of the window at its largest size at the scale 2, cut on each side to the screen of 1920 x 1080, because the desktop fits such a window into the screen before a scale applies; every other window is counted with no cut, as a window that is not resizable keeps its size at each scale. A resizable window of 1536 x 864 now asks for 4 050 pages of pictures in place of 10 368, and `paint` and `clock` fit again under the largest budget of a process.
- **A new window opens in a free place**: in place of a cascade that piled windows over each other, a new window takes the free place of the work area with the most room around it, whole on the screen. With no free place it takes a cascade place that covers no title, or the place that covers the fewest titles.
- **The recipe of an evaluated project is the recipe that `recipes` builds.** The new export recipes.register_evaluation takes the output of an evaluation whose value is a recipe, such as outputs.default of a project, and registers it under the hash that the evaluator wrote: the evaluator and the service compute it with one conversion. here() now gives a tree reference and module(...) a module file, each an input that names its kind, and declaration(here()) reads the declaration of the source tree. A bare hash in the arguments of a recipe, a builder, a project source or a console module given by a bare hash, an input below the first level, a source_date that is not an integer of 0 or more, and arguments whose record names are not sorted or appear twice are refused; the evaluator gives the failure Recipe_Refused with the words of the refusal.
- **The site is at modus.systems**: the pages, the feed and the sitemap name `https://modus.systems/` as their address, and the first address, `modus.froment.software`, sends each page to the same path there.
- **A failed line says the problem, not a number**: the console and `modustool call` no longer print the negative result of a failed export after its sentence, and the sentence of an error record says who can give a missing grant, says a status of the kernel in words ("a handle does not have the right that the operation needs"), and which address did not answer; a status or a kind that it cannot name stays on the detail lines, never as a number in the sentence. Each screen text now follows the guide `docs/WRITING.md`.
- **The words of the windows and the notifications.** Windows, notifications, context menus, action bars, buttons, and status lines follow `docs/WRITING.md`: buttons and labels use sentence case, the timer notification reads "The timer ended", the unlock window names the person or machine instead of "principal" and states the cause when key derivation or sealing is refused, `inspect`, `linux`, and the console replace "failed" alone and "invalid", and `settings.set_time_zone` reports the exact length, byte, range, or quarter-hour fault instead of "has the value 0".
- **Text with no size**: the editor `edit` holds a text of the size that its budget pays, in place of 32 KiB, and reads and writes it by ranges, so a log of 100 KB or a long source opens whole. A line of the console holds 4096 bytes in place of 200, with 256 levels of nesting, 4096 items in its lists and a history of 1024 lines kept across a stop; a variable keeps its value in memory of its size, and the console keeps 64 of them. The clipboard holds a copy of the size that the process that copies pays, in place of 4096 bytes, and the place that pastes takes what its room holds.
- **The last realisations of an object cache page over the width of their card.** Each recipe, output and signer hash is in its cell whole: the page shows the digits that its column holds, the whole 64 when they fit, and cuts the rest with an ellipsis, and a copy of the cell or its title gives the whole hash. A table of the site with a column of hashes takes the width of its box the same way.
- **The call outcome of the exports.** Each export that can fail gives its call outcome first and its value second, and the compiler makes each caller read the outcome: one word says whether the call succeeded, what it did to the state and what to do next, and a negative value is only a value. The console and `modustool call` keep the headline `error: <export>: <sentence>` and show `outcome` and `effect` as the first two detail lines under it, and the storage service answers with the same outcomes in place of its own negative numbers. A module in C includes `modus_call_outcome.h` and returns the pair `Modus_Answer`.
- **Programs of any size**: a module file of the store `module_files` has up to 64 MiB, and the store holds what its disk holds; `module_files` streams a file into the store by pieces, with no whole copy in its memory; a process holds 64 module records, and a module of the disk loads with up to 64 imports of the store on 16 levels, where 9 libraries were refused. A recipe takes 256 inputs and 64 KiB of arguments, and a build takes 4096 input objects. The C compiler takes a source of 64 MiB with 4096 headers and exports, and the assembler takes a text of 256 MiB, doubling its tables while a table is full and the budget of its call host pays.
- **Lists that page**: the document picker and the store picker of the powerbox read the documents and stores of a principal by pages with a search, so the 65th document and the 33rd store can be chosen; the palette gets the candidates of a field by pages; the dialog of a plan difference scrolls over each change, and its accept counts only after the principal saw the last line; and two machines exchange a store of any count of notes in pages, and objects of any size in pieces, past the 32 notes and the 16 KiB of before.
- **Storage that grows with its disk**: the storage service uses each block of its data disk, not its first 1 GiB, with its tables of the blocks in memory of its budget (6 bytes for each block, 114 MiB for a disk of 64 GiB); a disk holds up to 15 813 stores in pages of the store table, not 60; a name of an object or a tree has up to 255 bytes, not 64; a content tree holds 4096 entries of names of any length (the format `MODTREE2`), not 512, and the snapshot history of a store has the size of the memory of its caller. The format of the disk is `STORAGE5`: a disk of `STORAGE4` is not read.
- **The Linux window follows its size and keeps each pasted character.** The lines of the guest wrap at the width of the window, and the virtio console of the guest takes the columns and the rows of each resize. A paste longer than the input queue of the serial port waits in `linux` and reaches the guest whole and in order. Home, End and Delete reach the shell, the status line says when Linux starts, and each refusal says what did not happen, why, and what to do.
- **A window follows the screen, not a side of 2048 logical pixels**: a window can be up to 8192 logical pixels on a side, and the compositor bounds each surface by the screen in force, at twice its longest side and never less than 4096 pixels of the screen. On a screen of 5120 × 2880 at the scale 1 a window wider than 2048 opens, and a surface past the screen is refused as `Past_Screen`.
- **An image decodes whatever its sides, within the memory of its caller**: `modus:contract/image_decode` takes an image of any width and height up to 268 435 456 pixels, the pixels of one memory object, so a photo of 6000 × 4000 and a panorama of 30000 × 2000 decode. The output buffer that the caller gives bounds each image; a PNG wider than 4096 pixels takes its rows from the scratch, which its header names in `minimum_scratch_bytes`.
- **Larger guests and guest disks.** A guest process takes up to 32 guest processors. A guest build takes up to 16 GiB of guest memory, and input and output disks of up to 1 GiB with up to 4096 files, within the budget that its starter grants. A recipe of the guest builder names up to 32 inputs, and the trees of those inputs give the files of its input disk.
- **Paint opens a photo of a phone.** The picture of `paint`, its copy before a stroke and the bitmap of a fill are arenas of its budget, sized to the picture, in place of a fixed picture of 1536 × 864 in the pages of the module: a picture of 4096 × 4096 or a photo of 6000 × 4000 holds when the budget pays it, and the export `extend` grows the picture past the window. A picture that the budget does not pay is refused with a line of the log, and the picture stays. The file of a picture names the place of its rows, so a picture of any width keeps one whole picture at each commit; a file of before is refused as damaged.
- **The document of the browser in arenas.** The tree, the style sheet, the boxes and the records of a page grow with the page in arenas of the budget, so a page has no fixed count of nodes, rules or bytes of text, and a node index has 32 bits; the budget of the browser states a page of 1 MiB of HTML with 20 000 nodes. The fetch takes a body of the size of the memory that its caller gives it, in place of 16 KiB, and an address has up to 2048 bytes with a host of 253, a path and a query of 1024 each. The window keeps 256 pages of history and the state of 32 origins, each with 16 stored keys and 16 cookies.
- **A page of the server has a name of up to 64 bytes**: the name of a page is the name of its object in the store, so `server.put` takes the 64 bytes of a name of the store, in place of 39.
- **Cookies and a cache of the web client in memory that grows.** The cookie jar and the response cache of `modus:contract/http_client` hold the entries of any count of principals in memory of the caller that grows, with a quota for each principal that the caller sets, in place of 8 principals of 32 cookies and 8 cached answers of 4 KiB: a cookie has 4096 bytes of value, as RFC 6265 asks, and a cached answer has the size of its body. A principal still evicts only its own entries.
- **Notes as an application**: `notes` moves from the demonstrations to the applications group beside `assistant`, so the start menu lists it at the top under applications and its module category is `Application`. A refused write is tracked per note so only the note whose write failed shows "not kept", and an unreadable version error clears when Escape closes the versions pane.
- **The index of the glossary under the size bound of a page**: the glossary index lists each term as a compact row with its name, group and link to its entry on its group page, without repeating short definitions in the page body or in the JSON-LD page graph. Each `#term-<name>` anchor still lands on its row without JavaScript and redirects to the group entry with `glossary.js`, keeping the index well under half the 1 000 000-byte page bound even as the glossary doubles.
- **Notes of any size.** A note in `notes` has the size that the budget of `notes` pays, as a text of `edit`, not 32 KiB: the store thread reads and writes a large note in pieces, and keeps it as one version.
- **The console shows the whole edit line.** A line longer than one record of the terminal flows over the rows of the terminal, up to 4096 bytes after the prompt, in place of the part around the caret; a line taller than the view shows its rows down to the cursor, and the whole line keeps the colors of its code.
- **Builds whose modules pass 64 KiB**: the service `recipes` keeps each output in its private store by its hash, of up to 16 MiB, and removes its oldest realisations when its outputs would pass three quarters of its store, never an output that a recipe names as an input, and none at all when the removals cannot make room. A C build plans its memory from its source and from the budget that the service holds at the call, and the console reads a source by ranges, so `build` of a program of the size of Lua or SQLite gives its module when the budget pays it, and a build past the budget is refused with the pages that it needs.
- **Declaration files of 16 MiB**: a `.lex` file can have 16 MiB, where 64 KiB was refused. The evaluation sizes its node pool and its text arena from the files that it reads, so a short file costs what it did.
- **Storage calls end at a bound**: a call of the storage service that gets no answer in 120 seconds, twice the bound of the disk, ends with the status `No_Answer`, where the caller waited for ever. The next call waits for the late answer first, and the connection serves again when it came.
- **Every character that a font of the system has draws**: the em dash, the curly quotes, the ellipsis, the arrows, ≠, ≤, ≥, ∞, ș, ț, ł, č, œ, Vietnamese, the whole Greek and Cyrillic blocks and the currency signs now draw their glyph in each window and in the terminal, in place of the replacement box. The fonts of the boot image hold each character of their sources, 975 KB in place of 282 KB; a letter that B612 lacks takes the proportional Noto Sans in a label and keeps the cell of Paper Mono in the terminal. Armenian, Hebrew, Arabic, Thai, CJK and emoji still draw the box: no font of the system has them.
- **A machine runs hundreds of processes, and a large program gets a share of the memory.** The largest budget of a process of the boot image is a quarter of the memory and of the threads of the machine, at least 64 MiB, in place of 64 MiB for every machine. Each process holds 1024 handles and 1024 mappings, the root service holds 256 connections, the compositor holds 128 windows, and a start list holds 256 rows.
- **Remote and network for real use**: `remote` holds 32 clients at once and the client table 64 keys, 32 for one principal, which its disk grant table keeps across a restart (format 4: a table of an older format is refused); `remote_display` holds 8 viewers, its protocol takes screens up to 7680 × 4320 with the memory of the service and the bounds of a viewer frame from the screen in force; the network service holds 96 endpoints, 256 neighbors and a prefix length that it checks in place of a fixed /24; TCP scales its windows past 64 KiB (RFC 7323).
- **More clients for each service and for the network**: each service takes 48 callers at once (was 8), of which the clients of other machines take at most 32, and at most half of the connections of the root service, so a local console or window always connects; a connection of `remote` to a service ends when the last client of its key leaves or after 60 s with no call; the network service 96 endpoints, `remote` reaches 8 paired machines at once (was 2), 32 clients of `remote` and 8 viewers of `remote_display` connect at one time with no wait, and the QUIC server keeps the budgets of 1024 source ranges, with a prefix length that its limits set, in place of 64; a new range still connects when the table is full.
- **A disk grant table of an older format says what to do**: its warning line names the principal, says that its client keys and the grants of its disk processes do not hold, and tells the principal to pair each client again with `remote_allow` or `remote_display_allow`, and to give each disk process its grants again.
- **The kernel tables sized from the memory of the machine.** The kernel takes its tables once at its start, from the usable memory: one process slot for each 4 MiB, from 64 to 1024, with 4 threads and 64 memory objects for each slot. A machine of 1 GiB has 256 process slots, 1024 threads and 16384 memory objects for 48.9 MiB of tables, 4.8 % of its memory; a machine of 4 GiB or more has 1024 process slots. A log record names a process slot in two bytes, and `Inspect` gives the slots of the machine in `System_Report.process_slots`.
- **More threads in one process.** A process holds 256 threads, in place of 32, and a thread report gives all of them. The stack of the first thread starts at `0x7FFF_FFBD_D000`, below the 256 thread areas.
- **A wait set in the memory of the caller.** A `system.Wait` holds 64 words on the stack of its thread, about 3.5 KiB in place of 12 KiB, and a thread that waits on more sizes a `system.Wait_Set` to its tables, in its own memory. The kernel holds the words past 64 in a wait room of the thread, which the budget of its process pays, up to 32768 words: a new command `Thread_Control_Command.Wait_Room` gives the calling thread its room, the kernel writes its size in the thread record (`Thread_Record.wait_room_words`, in place of a reserved field), and the library grows it at the first wait with a larger set. A wait holds 64 words with no room, in place of 256. A word past the room is refused: the wait then ends only at its deadline, with one line on the log, and no loop turns with no wait. The compositor waits on each of its 128 windows, and the root service on each queue of each of its places, so it refuses no start for its wait.
- **The budget of the disk processes is stated.** All disk processes share 128 MiB, 4 processes and 16 threads, and one of them gets at most 64 MiB, on every machine: the budget never returns to the root service, so it no longer grows with the memory of the machine. The share of the machine for the largest budget of a process of the boot image has one border in the root service for a value of the plan of principal 0, from 1 to 64; no plan gives it yet, so it stays a quarter.
- **The call outcome in the log facts.** A fact holds a call outcome as a contract enum, written by the name of its member in the place of the earlier call status, so the facts of the tests, of the builders and of the measures name how a call ended in its own word.
- **The call outcome of the isolated call and of `remote`.** An isolated call, a call from the host through `remote`, a request between paired machines, the agent bridge and the calls of supervision now end with the same call outcome as a direct call, with the error record in the completion, so a caller tells a refusal from a call in doubt with one comparison. The answer of `remote` names the outcome of the request and the outcome of the export, and the agent bridge, at version 2 of its frames, names the state of a request and the outcome of a request that ended.
- **The last exports with a negative result give their call outcome.** `module_files.put` and `read`, `recipes.build_c`, `projects.new`, the act dialog of the compositor (`act_ask`, `act_poll`, `act_cancel`, `plan_diff_poll`) and the exports of the conversation of the assistant now give the call outcome first and the value second, as every other export does. A refusal of a recipe, of the debugger and of a step of the C builder now pairs its outcome with a kind that the outcome explains.
- **The call outcome of a role call between machines.** A role call through the proxy between paired machines, and an outbound request of `remote`, now end with the same call outcome as a call on one machine: the holder gives its outcome, which crosses the link, and a broken link or a passed bound gives an outcome in doubt, so a client of a remote role tells a refusal from a call in doubt as it does for a local holder.
- **Undo in paint has no count of steps.** The history of `paint` keeps the box that each change touched, in memory of the budget that grows up to one copy of the picture, and drops its oldest steps past that, in place of 64 steps in 1 MiB: a fill on a photo of 6000 × 4000 undoes. A change larger than that memory is kept as the one step of the history, in the copy before the stroke, in place of a change that could not be undone.
- **The names and the list of the pictures of paint follow the store.** A picture name is as long as a name of the store, less the suffix `.picture` (56 bytes today, 32 before), and the log keeps it whole. The panel of the pictures shows the store one page at a time, with the buttons previous and next, so a store with more than 64 pictures takes a new name; the store says if a name is free.
- **The full weight of a thread is 8192.** A weight is from 1 to 8192, in place of 1024, so a process of 256 threads still divides a process weight of 1/32 of the full weight between its threads. The process weight of `scheduler.share` is from 256 to 8192, and 8192 for a process with no setting: `scheduler.share("paint", 2048)` gives a quarter. Under the share policy a new thread has the process weight divided by 256, 32 for a process of the full weight.
- **The tables of the services sized from the process slots of the machine.** The root service takes its places, its wait set and its other tables for each process slot from its budget at its start, one for each process slot of the machine: 255 on a machine of 1 GiB, in place of 1023. The disk process page and the region of the devices service hold the rows of these places only, and `inspect` and `monitor` take their tables of the processes the same way.
- **A fill and a large step of undo in paint no longer stall the window.** The fill paints runs of a row in one pass, with a stack of runs that grows in the budget, in place of a fill of pixels: a sky of 22.8 million pixels fills in about 19 ms, 69 ms before. A fill, and the step of undo of a change larger than 262 144 pixels, run on a worker thread: the status line gives their progress, the window stays responsive, and Escape cancels them and puts the picture back.
- **A stroke in paint no longer copies the whole picture, and a large undo no longer stalls the window.** The copy of a picture before a change takes the tiles of 64 × 64 pixels that the change writes, at their first write, in place of the whole picture at each press. The undo, the redo and the clear of a large picture run on the worker of the changes, with their progress in the status line, and Escape cancels them with the picture as before.
- **A wake reads the waiters of its own word.** The kernel keeps the waited words in a bucket for each page frame and a list for each word, so a wake reads at most the 1024 words of one frame and the waiters of its word, whatever another process waits on. A `Word_Wait` that names a word twice is refused with `Bad_Operation` and the place of the word, and the library then waits once on each word and writes one line. A wait of more than 1024 words registers them in chunks, and frees the lock of its process between two.
- **The call outcome of the device rings and of the TPM ring.** The block, frame, network, entropy and TPM services now answer each request with a call outcome and an error record in place of a status set of their own, so one reader serves each of these rings and a caller tells a refusal from a failure of the device. A failed write or flush of a disk is a partial change, as some blocks can be on the disk; the iommu fact and the log facts of the network, the root service and `unlock` name the outcome.
- **A Linux shell that takes the size of its window.** The shell of the window `linux` runs on the virtio console `hvc0`, so a program of the guest gets `SIGWINCH` and the new size at each resize of the window. The log of the kernel stays on the serial port and goes to the log of the system only. The guest loads the virtio modules of the kernel of Nixpkgs from its initial archive.
- **The store picker shows the stores of the principal**: the trusted store picker (`powerbox_pick_store`) reads the stores of the principal of its caller from the storage service, by pages through the powerbox, in place of a fixed list of four names. A pick is kept only when the powerbox finds that store at its place in its own copy of the list; a list that changed after the picker showed refuses the pick, and the principal asks again.
- **The tables of the mesh have the size of the budget of `remote`**: the objects by hash, the exchanged stores and their versions grow as far as the budget of `remote` pays, keeping 512 pages for its other work, in place of 8 stores, 4 MiB of objects and 1536 pages of versions. A sync past the budget is refused at the page or the piece that needed the room, and the store keeps what it held. One store holds at most a quarter of the mesh, and one partner at most a half, the versions of its stores and the object that it sends together; a version or an object that a partner sent and that was refused gives its pages back at once, and so does an offer that stalled between two pages, at the next offer page of its partner or when its connection ends, and the store that the plan stops exchanging. A sync that stops between two pages ends with No_Answer, never as done.
