- **Pending changes of each connection**: each connection that writes ranges has its own pool of pending changes, bounded by the limit of blocks of its store, in place of 1024 blocks and 16 objects shared by every connection; a connection that writes a large range takes no room of another, and its commit writes all of its changes in one commit, of any size. The new export `storage.drop_pending(place, name)` drops the pending changes of one object and keeps the others of the connection.
- **The memory of the Linux guest follows its budget.** The window `linux` gives its guest the pages that its granted budget holds, past a reserve, from 128 MiB up; a smaller budget gives a window that says so in place of a guest.
- **The missing objects of a sync come by pages**: the list of the hashes that a machine lacks has any length, past the 64 of before, and a list with a repeat or out of order is refused whole.
- **A version reads in pieces**: `storage.version(place, name, generation, offset)` gives the bytes of a version from an offset, so a version larger than the call region reads whole, piece by piece; the client procedure `storage.version` reads all the pieces into its result.
- **A plan has any count of entries**: a plan, its difference and its dialog on the trusted display hold each entry, past the 32 entries and the 64 changes of before; a budget of the plans service that pays no more refuses the plan with `No_Memory`, and nothing changes. The trusted display receives each side of a difference in a memory object, and `plan_diff_ask` takes two `Bytes`.
- **A guest build fits the budget of the service.** Each guest build sizes its buffers from its spec and from the memory that the budget of the service has left, so a smaller machine runs the guest builds that fit it; a build whose guest does not fit, or whose kernel or initial archive the store lacks, is refused before any input is read.
- **A guest disk image holds paths of 255 bytes**: a file of the disk image of a guest build has a path of up to 255 bytes, one whole name of a store, in place of 124; the format version of the image is 2.
- **The cookies and the cache of the web client give each principal a part of its own.** The caller declares the principals that it serves, and each has a part of the memory of the size of its share, which the caller fixes (by default the room over those principals): a principal at its share replaces only its own least used cookie or cached answer, and one that fills its part never changes what another can hold or sees. A body past the share of a principal is refused before any entry goes.
- **The browser gives back the memory that a large page took.** After each page, the memory of the document keeps twice what the page needs and returns the rest to the budget, so a later page of another shape finds it.
- **No wait on device memory.** `Word_Wait` and `Word_Wake` refuse a word of a memory object of a device with `Bad_Address`, and the error record has `found` 2. No service of the system waits on a register: each waits on its rings and its interrupt events.
- **The call outcome of the root ring.** Each command of the root ring, and each wiring that the wiring service decides, now ends with a call outcome and an error record that the refusal names together, in place of a status of the kernel: a second pair of one source is `Name_Already_Taken` with `Exists`, a full wiring ring `Callee_Busy` with `Busy`, a revocation of a grant that the process does not hold `Nothing_Has_That_Name` with `Absent`. The console, the debugger, `remote`, the start policies and `system.service_connection` read that outcome, and the log facts of a start that did not run name it.
- **A plan difference is read off the interface thread**: the trusted display shows the dialog of a difference at once with "Reading the difference" and stays responsive while a worker reads the lines of a difference of any size; the plans service gives the two sides in one memory object that the display keeps with Read only until the worker read it. `plan_diff_ask` takes that object and the byte counts of its two sides in place of two `Bytes`.
- **One consent arm time.** Each consent dialog, the grant dialog of the powerbox and the plan and act dialogs of the compositor, takes a press only 500 ms after it shows. A principal can lengthen the time of the compositor dialogs with `consent_arm_time`, never shorten it.
- **The show time of a notification has a border.** A notification shows from 1 to 60 seconds, 8 seconds by default; a time outside that range is refused with `Show_Time_Out_Of_Range` in place of a silent change.
- **The settings keep only what the principal chose.** The store of `settings` holds a name and a word for each value that the principal changed, so a default that the principal never touched follows a later release. A word that a setting refuses holds as no choice and stays in the record, and a record that this release cannot read is no longer written over at boot. `reset` makes one choice absent again, and `settings_state` gives the layer of each value.
- **The range and the choices of a setting in its description.** Module files are version 8: an Integer, a Handle or an Enum parameter can be optional, as a presence word then its value, and a parameter carries its range and the exports that give its choices and its instances. The console refuses a number out of its range before the call, and `compositor.set` takes the five settings of the display, the numbers included, in one call. Files of version 7 are refused.
- **The presentation record on the window ring.** A window no longer maps the settings page of the machine: the compositor sends it the font, the order of the stripes, the grid fit, the appearance in force, the scale, the time zone and its first language in the display event `Presentation`, which replaces `Settings_Changed`, and the window keeps them on a page of its own. The pointer theme, the layout of the keyboard and the rest of the language list stay with the holders that use them.
- **A script font set of up to 255 fonts**: a reader takes the count of fonts from the header of the set, and the cache of glyphs and each direct glyph name a font in 9 bits, so a new script adds its font to the set with no change of the readers. A set with more fonts than the room of its reader is refused with the sentence `their count of fonts is past the room of the reader`.
- **A call refused for a full audit says why.** When the act records of a wide grant have no room left, `remote` refuses each call with the kind `Full` of the unit `Act_Records`, not `Busy`. The card of the mark counts the acts refused for that cause apart from the input out of the picked windows.
- **A start list refuses terms that name no audit of a server.** A wiring row whose terms name another kind of space, a forwarding, or a bound or a record size out of range is refused when the start list is read, with `Bad_Terms`.
- **A call of the library module text_rendering uses at most 16 KiB of stack**: a caller on a worker thread of 64 KiB keeps three quarters of it. The reader of CFF reads each font dictionary when it draws a glyph, the measure of a font reads its outlines with at most 256 points, and a font with CFF outlines in the place of a font of the boot image is refused, in a process and in a call alike. A host test measures the stack of each export.
- **Each boot volume fits again**: the shaping of marks, Arabic, Thai, the scripts of India and lines of mixed direction, with the script font set and its CFF fonts, is now in the library module `text_rendering`, the drawing module and the root service only. Each other module places a text of simple characters itself and calls the library module for each other line, so the boot image is 31.2 MB in place of 37.6 MB, and the boot program with its debug image fits the volume of 64 MiB.
- **The collector pays the memory of each space from its budget, and keeps room for a wide grant.** An audit space now takes its memory when it opens and gives it back when it closes, so a collector holds as many spaces as its budget pays, up to 16, not a fixed 8. The three act spaces of a wide grant always keep their room: a server audit that would take it is refused with the kind `Budget`. An image whose start lists declare more audits than the collector can hold is refused at build.
- **An audit space whose record size changed closes and keeps its records.** When a module changes the size of an audited record, its old audit space takes no more records, and its records stay readable and verifiable, across a restart. A new space takes the new records.
- **The floor of a module, counted whole, replaces the budget that it asked for.** The table `Floor` of each module with an entry holds what its process needs to reach its first state of work: its modules and their load (frame lists and page tables), its threads, its window, its start objects (`MODULE_START_OBJECTS`, `PACKAGE_START_OBJECTS`) and the floors of the children that the module table names, with the working memory that is left. A file with no table `Floor` no longer starts with a least budget: its start is refused with one line.
- **The floors of the start list first, and a size of twice the floor.** The root service reserves the floor of each row of its start list before the first row starts and divides the pages left by one rule, so the start order decides no size; a process gets twice the floor of its start by default, at most a share of the machine, and a floor that the paying budget cannot give refuses the start with one line that names both amounts. The build proves that the floors of each start list fit in a machine of 1 GiB.
- **A collector holds as many audit spaces as its budget pays.** The collector no longer stops at 16 windows and 32 places: its spaces, its table and its index grow with its budget. The root service gives the collector of principal 0 the memory of each audit that the start list declares, so 20 audited servers get 23 windows with the act spaces. A start list is refused at build only when its audits would pass the largest budget of one process.
- **The principal identifier in the file**: each new named principal gets 32 random bytes that name it on the disk, kept in its file under the seal and given to the root service, which starts no session with no identifier. A file of an older release is refused with the sentence "the file has the format of an older release of modus, which this release does not read: create the principal again".
- **Wirings and stream ends have no fixed count.** An asker is no longer stopped at 8 wirings, the machine at 24, or a process at 16 stream ends: each holds what its budget pays, so a collector can read 20 audited servers. A wiring that the budget does not pay is refused with the kind `Budget` of the unit `Pages`, and nothing changes.
- **A start list holds as many wiring rows as it needs, and its start policy is paid for them.** The list no longer stops at 8 wiring rows. Its header gives the pages that its wirings cost, and the root service adds them to the budget of the start policy, which no longer keeps a fixed reserve of 64 pages. The build refuses a writer with more rows than one start holds, and a list whose pages pass the largest budget of one process.
- **The data disk format is `STORAGE7`.** The top directory of the count tree records the leaves to recount. A disk of an earlier format is refused whole with a sentence that says so, and is never taken for a new disk to format.
- **The principal identifier in the stores, the pin and the audit**: each store of a named principal names its owner by its principal identifier in its store block, and keeps only a name tag in its cell, so a reader of the disk reads no name of it; the pin of the file of a principal and the index of its collector name the identifier too. A data disk of an older release is refused with "the data disk has the format of an older release of modus, which this release does not read: write the data disk again", and the audit spaces of an older collector with a sentence of their own.
- **A collector reads as many sources as its budget pays.** It no longer stops at 16 sources: 20 audited servers and a wide grant are 23 sources. A source that the budget does not pay is refused, and nothing changes.
- **The wiring budget of a process is stated, and pays its entries.** A module states the pages of its wiring rings in its budget (`wiring_pages`); the root service gives it that amount at its first wiring, or half of its pages when it states none, whatever it spent before. The entries that its wirings take in the wiring service are paid from it, so one process cannot fill the wiring service for the others.
- **A reader waits on each of its rings**: a record input, the collector, the log and the wiring service wait on every ring that they hold, in a wait that grows with their tables and a wait room that the budget pays; no ring is read again on a period of 10 milliseconds, and the fact `Poll_Wakes_Counted` of the collector is gone.
- **Higher bounds for TLS handshakes, grant revocations, assistant act lines, and box-tree layouts for assistant and manual.** TLS 1.3 over TCP reassembles multi-record handshakes up to 65535 bytes without a stack copy, signed grant revocation lists grow past 64 inline entries up to 65535 serials with fail-closed scratch verification, `text_model` records slower-tier growth past its 32 KiB inline buffer, assistant act lines accept up to 4096 bytes matching the console line editor, and the `assistant` and `manual` windows lay out through `modus:layout` with dark and light element tokens.
- **Removed UI hardcoded caps and migrated seven desktop windows to `modus:layout`.** `Box_Tree` (1024 inline boxes), `Interface_State` Tab focus order (64 inline elements), and `note_list` (256 inline notes) now move transparently to caller-supplied overflow tiers with transition counters, while note titles and `document_picker` names accept the full 255-byte STORAGE7 limit. `clock`, `paint`, `processors`, `locks`, `scheduler`, `hall_of_fame`, and `terminal` now compute their window layouts with `modus:layout` and `Element_Tokens`, verified by host non-overlap tests across window sizes, HiDPI scales, and dark and light appearances.
- **Session-scoped services, display isolation at lock, and closed joint-process check.** Every start grant set declares explicit `.Session` or `.Machine` scope checked against a closed 15-process joint list, session services launch per principal with encrypted stores, and locking a session isolates display state by restarting the display holder (or resetting clipboard, notifications, palette recents, and menus when a machine window stays open).
- **The call outcome of the kernel.** Each completion of a kernel operation now holds a `call_outcome.Call_Outcome` in place of `operation.Status`, so calls to the kernel and calls to services end with the same outcome set; `operation.Status`, `system.Status`, `system.outcome_of_status`, `system.outcome_of_own_status` and `Modus_Status` are removed, and property 14 names `Budget_Or_Capacity_Exhausted` with `Budget`.
- **The test image carries its own plan file.** Test boot images ship the signed `test_plan_values` resource carrying the budget rows for `limits` and `log_flood`, release boot images exclude it, and the root service removes its hardcoded fallback exception tables.
- **Each wait for another process ends at its stop or at the hang bound.** Replaced the remaining short fixed wait timeouts across the console (`request_record`, `disk_request`, `WIRE_ANSWER_LIMIT`, palette provider refresh and revoke wait), `debugger`, `remote` mesh handshakes and requests, `root` disk load cancel, `compositor` GPU scanout (with immediate fallback when `gpu_device` stops), and `quic_server` endpoint info waits with serving-process stop checks and the 60 s answer hang bound.
- **Self-contained chaptered `modustool film tutorial` built around `db_browser`.** Replaced the raw `collatz` + hash-typing screencast with a seven-chapter tutorial featuring spliced 1920x1080 title cards, cue-driven non-overlapping captions rendered with modus fonts, live `db_browser/project.lex` shown in `edit`, typed `Project_Build` and `Provenance` console records (zero hand-typed 64-hex hashes), the desktop `grants` window, live SQLite queries, and copy-on-write version restore into a <= 4 MiB AV1 WebM and H.264 MP4.
- **Public object cache page updated for TCP and QUIC.** The public object cache status page and `status.json` now report both TCP and QUIC (UDP) support on port 5010, matching the deployed `cache.modus.systems` instance.
- **Fast-first static checks and split cached Nix check derivations.** `modustool check` now runs all fast static checks before host tests, fuzzing, and the second build, and adds composable sub-modes (`static`, `host_tests`, `fuzz <shard> <total>`, `reproducible`). `flake.nix` splits `modus-host-checks` into narrow, independently cached derivations (`modus-static-checks`, `modus-system-host-tests`, `modus-tool-host-tests`, four `modus-fuzz-*` shards, `modus-reproducible-build`, and `modus-scenario-waits`) so documentation, site, and film edits fail fast and skip unrelated fuzzing and rebuilds.
- **One-step `plans.apply` / `plans.rollback` and named status columns in plans and recipes tables.** `plans.apply("name", plan { ... })` and `plans.rollback([n])` in the console now auto-connect to `plans`, validate the plan up front, wait for trusted display consent on the background command thread (with Escape cancellation), and return the committed `Plan_Generation_Row` directly without manual `apply_poll` / `rollback_poll` calls or a mandatory `{}` third argument. Status fields in `plans` and `recipes` records (`standing`, `consent`, `output_kind`, `kind`, `outcome`, `refusal`, `mode`, `origin`) now use contract enums so tables display names such as `Current`, `Earlier`, `Built_Locally`, and `Tree` instead of raw integers.
- **Bound, namespaced console module handles.** `load`, `isolate`, and `isolate_memory` now require binding to a variable (`m = load("math")`, `s = isolate("sample")`) and register exports and record constructors strictly under `<binding>.<name>`. Multiple instances of the same module can run simultaneously, `help(s)` lists the commands of one handle, and `unload(s)` or reassigning `s` removes only that handle's commands and stops its call host or releases its module.

### Removed

- **The export `compositor.wide_grant_indicator`.** The mark of a wide grant has one writer, the root service, so no process that acts for the principal shows or clears it.

### Fixed

- **The pointer of `modustool run` follows the pointer of the host.** The machine of `modustool run` has a virtio tablet, and the root service now gives the tablet to the input service: the pointer moves to where the host pointer is, and the window of QEMU no longer grabs the pointer of the host.
- **Maximize fills the work area.** The maximize button, the menu entry and Control+Shift+Up now give a resizable window the size of the screen less the bar, within the limits of the window, and the second toggle gives back its place and its size; a snap to a half of the screen resizes the window too.
- **The row `settings` of a start list reaches the holder of the role Settings.** The boot pass started the module `settings` of the boot image by its name, so a plan of principal 0 that gave the role to another module did not reach it; it now starts the holder in force and gives it the value of the boot pass. That holder still gets the grants of its own module name, not those of `settings`.
- **A press reaches the element under the pointer in every window**: the windows of `settings`, `address_space`, `interface_font` and `vector_glyphs` drew their frames with no scale while each press came in logical pixels, so at the scale 1.25 a press on `+15 min` in `settings` chose `dark`, and its content filled four fifths of its window. Each frame now takes the scale of its window, and `interface.window_frame_start` refuses at compile time a frame with no window.
- **The palette goes to each setting of the window of settings**: a window gives 12 act names in place of 6, as hashes of 32 bits, so the scale, the seventh setting, is no longer dropped with the line `a frame names 1 act names that the window does not keep`.
- **Console tables break their texts between words.** A table wider than the console window narrows its columns of text, and a long text goes on under its own column, broken between two words, as `help()` shows its descriptions; only a word longer than its column is cut. A long text of a record goes on under its value.
- **Text in Spleen is whole and reads as words.** With the font setting Spleen, a small text lost the last column of its last letter ("a seconc" in the monitor), and a heading stood as spaced letters ("m a n u a l"); each character now takes the width of its Spleen glyph, in the measure and in the draw, in every window.
- **The plates of the bar keep their places.** The plates follow the order in which the windows opened, so a change of the focus no longer moves them under the pointer.
- **The web console starts in seconds**: the kernel of the browser machine copies memory with one `memory.copy` in place of a loop of one byte, and the software processor copies its registers at each instruction. In Node on a loaded host, the console prompt came at 12.9 s in place of 76.6 s.
- **The web desktop reaches its first frame**: on a browser machine the root service checks the table of the boot image at its start and hashes each file at its first load or give, once, and refuses a file whose hash differs before any read of it; on a machine whose boot program checked the files, it hashes each one once at its start, as before, and no load or give hashes it again. The compositor validates only the pointer theme in force, and runs its measures of copies, title bar, arrow and bar only in a measure run, after its first frame. Before, the start of the desktop in a page passed the hang bound of the start policy, and the rescue start policy stopped it before its first frame.
- **The trusted display shows the difference of a plan to the holder of Plans.** `plans.apply` and `plans.rollback` were refused with `Display_Refused` on each machine, because the display served only a caller that acts for the principal and `plans` acts for nobody. The root service now names the holder of the role Plans in the grant of its connection to the display, and writes that grant again when the roles change, so a module that lost the role is refused.
- **Each corner of a window resizes it on the diagonal.** The grip at the bottom right and the 14 pixels along each edge from a corner take both edges, with the diagonal pointer shape.
- **The trust record says who checks the files**: a new field `file_check` names the boot program, the root service at its start, or the root service at the first use of each file. A browser machine writes the root service at the first use and no verifier, and the root service writes on the log that no boot program verified the description, in place of the false lines "verified by the boot program" and "checked the signature and every hash in 0 us".
- **The getters of the Settings contract give an enum.** The contract of the role Settings named `font`, `appearance` and `scale` with an Integer result, while `settings` gives a member of a closed set: the root service refused `settings` itself, and each other holder, as a holder that a plan names. The contract now names an Enum result.
- **The role contracts read whole on the machine.** The tables of the role contracts sliced other tables in their initializers, which only the start of a program fills, and a module has no such start: on the machine the root service read eleven contracts with no exports and refused each holder that a plan named, while each host test passed. The tables are now constant data, `modustool rule-checks` refuses such an initializer in each source of the system, and the machine test `fails` compares the hash of each contract on the machine with the hash on the host.
- **The preview of a group on the bar closes**: Escape, a press outside it, a window that opens, closes or comes to the front, and the pointer that leaves the plate and the card now close it. It stays while the pointer goes from the plate onto the card, and a card that was closed does not come back until the pointer leaves its plate.
- **The palette says when Enter runs nothing**: with no entry chosen, the detail line says "nothing to run", and the panel stays open with what was typed, until the next edit.
- **Each stop of the scale says its percent.** The track of the scale in the window of settings shows 100, 113, 125 up to 200 in its stops at each width that holds three digits. The scale in force is the filled stop, with its label in the colour of a chosen value, and the focus is an outline on a stop that keeps its colour: the focus never looks like the choice.
- **A change of settings that its store refuses changes nothing.** A change is kept in the private store of settings first, then shown: when the store refuses it, the desktop keeps the values before, and the call fails with Refused. Before, the desktop showed a value that the next start of the machine lost. A settings process that gets no store says so once at its start.
- **The first call to a service that starts on demand can lend a handle**: the call failed with "an argument names no handle of the console" when the service had not mapped its call ring yet. A call host or a service now sends a ready completion once it can receive handles, and each caller waits for it before its first call.
- **A slow start no longer fails a correct connection**: the wait for the answer of the root service to a connection, and for a service or a call host to become ready, ends when that process stops, or at the hang bound of 60 s. It ended after 3 to 30 s before, which a loaded machine can pass while it starts a service.
- **The measure of a small damage of the remote display at each scale.** `remote_display` and the machine test count a viewer frame as a small damage when it holds at most 1/64 of the screen. The bound was 64 by 64 pixels: at the scale 1.25 the clock of the bar gives viewer frames of 4824 pixels, so no frame counted, the service never wrote its measure line, and the test part `remote_display` failed at `small frames` while the viewer received one frame each second.
- **One service that starts slowly no longer holds every client of `remote`**: the connection to a service is now a state of the loop of `remote`, so the calls to the other services go on while one service starts, and a service that never answers ends its connection at the hang bound.
- **Escape stops a line that waits for a service**: a line of the console that connects to a service that starts slowly now ends at Escape, as an isolated call does. An Escape no longer reaches the calls of the palette after the end of its line.
- **The names `module_files` and `grant_table` are refused with a true sentence**: `projects.new`, `module_files.put` and `module_files.receive` say that the store has an entry with this name already, and `module_files.read` says that no module file has it. They said that the name was a process of the system.
- **The flags of a device region are written under the lock of its frames.** A region of a device that a guest maps no longer has its cache flags written outside that lock, so a concurrent read of the flags sees either the old or the new value.
- **A module file is replaced whole**: `module_files.receive` and `module_files.put` write a file into a staging object and make its nickname name it in one commit, so a body that ends early leaves the program before working, one file makes one version, and a failed write drops its pending changes in the storage service. The service removes each staging object that a stop left, at its start and before a listing. A receive ends when no piece of 32 KiB comes for 30 seconds, or past a total of 30 seconds and its bytes at 64 KiB each second.
- **A device window returns when its object is no longer declared.** A memory object gives its device window back after the last end of its declarations, once each domain dropped its pages, in place of at its end: a service that declares many objects in turn no longer fills the 2047 windows. A declaration with no free window gives `No_Slot`, and its sentence names the device windows.
- **A commit on a damaged object keeps its pending changes.** When `storage.commit` finds the state before damaged, it gives `Data_Damaged` and the pending changes of the connection stay, as the outcome says that nothing changed; only a commit after another change of the object drops them.
- **The scaled window of TCP stays at its buffer**: with window scaling, the window that the machine gives a remote rounds up from its free room by less than one unit of its scale, a full buffer gives a window of 0, and a byte past the free room of the buffer is not taken.
- **A guest build ends on a time bound, and the service answers while it runs.** A guest that never exits by itself is ended by the time bound of its spec, at most one day, and refused with `Builder_Timed_Out`; before, it held the service `recipes` for ever. A guest build runs on a thread of its own: `recipes.build` answers `Building`, and the service answers each other call while the guest runs. Guest builds wait in a queue of 8, at most 2 for each principal, in turns between principals, and `recipes.build_cancel` cancels a build of the principal that asks.
- **The store of recipes keeps only what something names.** A failed guest build removes the objects that it wrote. A pass at the start of the service and after each guest build removes each object that no realisation, no recipe and no recent put names, and keeps each object under a named tree. A principal keeps at most 256 objects with `recipes.put` in one hour, whichever connection it uses. Objects larger than one call of the store, as a kernel or a large output file, are written and read in parts with one commit.
- **An undo or a cancel in paint gives back each pixel.** A long, shallow or steep line, and a text that the console writes during a stroke, wrote pixels that the copy before the stroke did not hold, so an undo or a cancel left them changed; each pixel that a stroke writes is now in the copy. A long clear of a large picture stops at Escape between two bands, not only at its end.
- **SARA AM after 16 marks**: a consonant of Thai or Lao with 16 marks, then SARA AM, keeps SARA AM whole; before, the shaper wrote one mark past the marks of the cluster. The shaper reads each byte of a text once for its cluster, so a text that another thread changes during a call gives other glyphs, never a write past a table.
- **A mark of class 0 blocks the composition**: a letter, then a mark of class 0 that composes with nothing (U+034F), then other marks, keeps each of those marks apart, as UAX #15 says. Before, a mark after it could still compose with the letter: e, U+034F, U+0323 and U+0301 drew é.
- **Arabic joins through an invisible format character**: a left-to-right mark, a zero width space or a word joiner between two letters of Arabic keeps their joined forms, as their joining type Transparent says. Before, the letters drew as if a space stood between them.
- **The refusals that changed something say so.** A command or a request that changed what its caller can see and then failed now ends with `Stopped_After_A_Partial_Change`: an extend of PCR 12 whose read back failed, a pin of the TPM after its index changed, a start whose wiring commit did not leave, a held debug start that stopped. A revocation of `Store` or `History` checks the room of each service ring before it changes anything. A foreign sealed object and a wrong passphrase at the TPM are `Argument_Refused` (`Not_Authentic`), a replacement row that does not verify is `Data_Damaged`, a locked session of a remote client is `Wrong_State`, and a refusal of the receiver of an isolated call names the unit `Region` or `Lent_Handles` with its amounts in bytes of the call region. The console, `unlock` and the test modules write these outcomes by their names.
- **A change of a pin that the TPM can have run says it is in doubt.** When the interface fails after a removal, a definition or a write of the index of a pin reached the TPM, or its answer cannot be read, the request ends with `Deadline_Passed_During_The_Call` or `Answer_Unreadable`, as an extend of PCR 12 does, not with `Callee_Unavailable`. A commit of ranges that has no pending change, or that lost the race with another change, now says so in plain words: "the object has no pending change on this connection", or "another change of the object came first since its first pending change, so its pending changes are dropped".
- **One store that cannot show no longer hides the store picker.** A store whose name is no display text of 1 to 64 bytes, or that repeats a name or a number, is left out alone, and the log names it with its cause; the other stores of the principal show. A principal who opens and leaves several pickers fast leaves no dialog of the powerbox open.
- **Brackets in a line of mixed direction**: a pair of brackets takes the direction of the text inside it (UAX #9, N0), so a closing bracket after right-to-left text in a line of left to right no longer flips to the other side. A paragraph separator (U+2029) and the white space before it take the direction of the paragraph (L1).
- **A plan with no script font set gives back the set of the boot image**: after a plan of principal 0 named a set, a plan that names none makes the set of the boot image the set of the machine again, at once before the read of this start, else at the next start, with its line in the log. Before, the set of the earlier plan stayed.
- **The line of a script font set that the storage service does not answer states its bound**: the line and the error record name the deadline of the load of the set, 16 times that of a module file, not the deadline of a module file.
- **The records of a wide grant stopped after 32 acts and 16 grants.** The trusted display now writes one record for each change of the window of the input, with the counts of the keys and the presses; the space of each writer is sized to the grant, keeps thousands of records in the store and its newest in memory, and the oldest closed grants rotate out to make room, so a grant of an hour and the seventeenth grant keep their records.
- **The end of a wide grant could be lost.** The revocation in the collector is sent again until it is answered, the record of a lock keeps its reason before the collector stops, a grant that the collector created after its flow ended is revoked, the end time is the same in the root service and in the collector, a grant whose mark did not show ends, and the pause after a refused screen is kept for each key, with the proofs not given counted for each principal until it unlocks.
- **Every pair of brackets of a line takes part in its direction**: the rule N0 of UAX #9 no longer keeps the first 63 pairs and silently leaves the others neutral; it keeps every pair that a line of 512 glyphs can hold, so a line with many brackets keeps its order of mixed direction.
- **Lam and alef before a Thai SARA AM**: after the ligature of lam and alef, NIKHAHIT draws once and SARA AA stands at the byte of SARA AM, as after any other letter.
- **No consonant of a syllable of the scripts of India vanishes**: a run of viramas and joiners at the bound of a syllable no longer drops the consonant after it, and a reph with no consonant after it draws as its letters.
- **An audit space is read whole, and its collector starts with a few reads.** A reader of an audit space now reads each record that it keeps, the oldest ones from the store, not only the newest 64. A collector reads a few objects for each space at its start, whatever its count of records, and its start line gives that count. The memory of the collector no longer limits the bounds of its spaces: the act spaces of a wide grant and several audits of servers fit at once.
- **The act records of a wide grant miss no window and no view.** The keys and presses of the last window of a viewer are recorded at the end of the grant and every 10 seconds while it holds. A view that is open when a grant starts gets its record then. A grant that a newer one replaces closes each of its rings.
- **A new key pauses after a refused screen when the table of keys is full.** When each row of the table holds a key barred for the run, the refusals of new keys count together, so a new key cannot ask again with no pause.
- **A changed or lost part of an audit store is found.** A collector refuses to start on a lost or older map of the segments of an audit space, where it read fewer records before. A reader that reads old records up to the newest ones in memory finds old segments rewritten from another chain.
- **The counts of the input of a viewer never close the ring of the trusted display.** The display writes the waiting key and press counts before each of its other records, so their time never goes back and the collector keeps them.
- **A refused read keeps the choices of the settings.** When the store did not give the kept settings, the next change wrote a new record with that change alone and lost each other choice; the settings now read the record again first, and refuse the change while the store does not give it.
- **A refused change of record size changes nothing.** When the memory of the new space or the write of the index is refused, the old space of a module stays open with its records, and takes the next ones.
- **The blocks that a wrong release keeps are freed on a disk larger than the memory.** A release or a dropped version that meets a wrong block now leaves a record of the leaves to recount, and the log says the blocks it keeps. The recount starts when free space falls under a sixteenth of the disk, or on `storage.recount()` from the console, which holds the new start grant `Recount`; it starts only when the disk has room for its tally, and after a recount that found no room none starts until free space grows. It is one walk of the stores, with its names sorted by leaf, during which reads are served and changes get `Busy` with a sentence that names the recount and its progress, then one commit for each directory of leaves that differ. A loss of power keeps the leaves still to recount.
- **A removed audit space leaves no segment.** The end of a closed audit space removes the segments that had rotated out too, so a new space in its place starts clean, also after a restart.
- **The floors and their reserve hold every start.** A ready ring or a root ring whose counters say more records than it holds now ends with one line, where the root service looped with no wait; the floor of a module now holds the wiring budget that the root service carves and the fixed budgets that it gives its children before its first state of work; each row of the start list keeps its divided size until its start succeeds, and no longer past the end of the list; and the build proves that every start of the root service, not the list alone, fits in a machine of 1 GiB.
- **A boot volume holds its files**: the tool sizes each boot volume to its boot program and its debug image, from 64 MiB, the least of FAT32, upward; a volume no longer fails to build when the two pass 64 MiB together.
- **The debug image bound is computed from free memory.** The boot program refused a debug image past 32 MiB, and the debug image of the build had grown to 34 MB: the machine then started with no names in its stop reports and in the debugger. The boot program now reads a debug image up to one quarter of free physical memory (capped at 1 TiB), leaving memory for the kernel and root service.
- **The rows of the start list keep their pages from every other start.** The share that the root service keeps beside the rows now holds the floors of each start that can come before the list is done, not one largest budget; the budget of the disk processes and the growth of a screen take no page that a row keeps, and a disk process refused before `Machine_Usable` names the start-list reserves as the cause. A ring that the root service reads with a false count logs once per ring, and a ready ring that wakes with no record ends with one line, where the root service looped. A module that holds the root ring and never wires no longer gets a wiring part in its floor.
- **Each holder of the boot image fills the contract of its role.** Every member of the role catalog now has a canonical contract and BLAKE3 hash (including Assistive, Speech, Block_Device, Network_Device, Sound_Device, Entropy_Device, and Tpm), and all 21 default boot-image holders pass contract verification in host tests.
- **Service, picker, disk-load, and role-update waits use the 60 s hang bound instead of short fixed timeouts.** Raised `remote.CALL_LIMIT`, `console.LINE_RECORD_ANSWER_LIMIT`, `root.PICKER_LISTING_BOUND_MILLISECONDS`, `powerbox.STORES_CALL_BOUND_MILLISECONDS`, `disk_process.LOAD_DEADLINE_MILLISECONDS`, and `plans.ROLES_BUSY_PAUSE_MILLISECONDS` to match `ANSWER_BOUND_MILLISECONDS` (60 s), and removed the 1000 ms polling waits in `remote`'s weak-seed loop.

### Security

- **A wake needs Write on its word.** `Word_Wake` now refuses, with `Bad_Address`, a word that the process can read and not write, and its error record says that the process can read it (`found` 1). Before, a process that maps a page read-only, such as the date page, the settings page or an end page of a wiring, could wake each thread of another process that waits on a word of that page: a signal between two processes that share no right to write. A wait still needs Read only.
- **The pointer of a viewer on a trusted dialog.** A viewer of the remote display with the input could press the dialog of the difference of a plan; the pointer and the keys of a viewer now reach no dialog of the trusted display and no window of `unlock` that proves a wide grant.
- **A page of the browser reads no byte of the page before it.** A page whose order of nodes was cut left the nodes past the cut in the memory of the document, where a later page of another origin could reach them; the memory is now wiped to each byte that a page wrote.
- **An unmap wakes the threads that wait on its frames.** A thread that waited on a word of a memory object that its process then unmapped and closed kept waiting on a frame that returned to the free frames: the next process that took the frame had its wakes of count 1 taken by that thread, and the thread learned its wakes. The unmap, and the end of the point copies of a record, now wake such a thread first, as a decommit and a revocation did. A refused `Word_Wait` now leaves its whole entry with no wait.
- **The command of a guest build runs as an unprivileged user.** The `init` of a guest build runs the command as user 1000, with no access to the ports of the build or to the disks, so the command cannot report a status or write the output disk in place of `init`; each process that it leaves ends before `init` reads `/outputs`, and no link there is followed.
- **A caller can no longer stop the trusted display with a memory object that it gives.** The display refuses an Uncommitted object of a plan difference at its border, and reads a difference and the entries of the palette by a copy of the kernel, never through a mapping: a page with no frame, or a revocation by the giver during the read, ends the dialog with no decision, or leaves the palette without the entries of its provider, and the desktop keeps running. The display makes each window surface itself, with a budget that the window gives for that surface alone, and gives the window a copy with Read and Write: `Surface_Create`, `Surface_Resize` and `Surface_Rescale` carry that budget and the size, the answer names the surface, and `display.open` waits for it; an open that fails leaves no window, and the next one makes it. The memory of the capture face comes the same way, with the new event `Capture_Opened`; the role contract of the display is version 3. So the display maps no memory that a window made, and no window can take back a mapping of the display. A paste into the palette reads the copied text by the kernel too. The palette table keeps its header, its result and its candidates in the table that the root service made; the entries that pass it move to an object of the entries alone (`Table_Replaced`, table version 10).
- **An act of an agent ran with no record.** Under a wide grant, a key, a press, a view and a call of `remote` ran when the ring of their record had no reader or had ended; each now runs only once its record left, a writer that starts during a grant is wired before its acts run, and a ring that closes ends the grant.
- **The input of a viewer reached a window that the principal did not pick.** A window that opened in the place of a closed window of a wide grant took its keys, and an open palette, menu or card over a picked window took them too; each window now has an id that is never given again, a closed window leaves the grant, the input of a viewer goes only to the surface of a picked window with nothing over it and only with the scope to send input, one trusted dialog shows at a time, and the screen names each window by the name of its module.
- **A hostile font no longer holds a shaping call**: one budget of work bounds a whole call, in proportion to its text (2 048 units for each byte, four times the measured need, 65 536 at least and 67 108 864 at most), across each stage, each test of a feature, each form of a letter of Arabic, each anchor of a mark and each record that a lookup names, the skipped records too; past it, the rest of the text draws with the plain glyphs of its characters. A script font whose tables pass the bound of their walk at its open draws the replacement glyph, and the process says so once.
- **The grant dialog waits for its arm time.** The grant dialog of the trusted display took Enter at once, so a key meant for a window could grant a network endpoint or a store share; it now takes a grant only after the arm time that the principal chose, never under 500 ms, as the difference of a plan does, and the powerbox service checks that time in the answer.
- **The store of a role goes to its holder only.** A module named as a role that a plan gave to another module, such as settings, reached the store of the role and the choices of the principal in it; its connection to the storage service is now refused.
- **The log by principal**: a console or `remote` that acts for a principal reads, with `log.records`, `log.value_runs` and `log.fact_records`, the records of the processes of that principal only, and principal 0 also the records of the kernel and of the root service; before, it read the whole log, with the records of each other principal.
- **One authority key of the wide grants for each principal**: the collector of a principal receives a key of that principal only, so it can make no proof of a wide grant that the collector of another principal accepts; before, each collector received the one key of the machine.
- **The log by principal, whole**: `log.follow` and the window of the log service give each reader the records of its principal only, a line of the root service about a session is a record of that session, and a reader of a principal sees each record with its number among the records of that principal, so no number tells it how many records the others wrote.
- **The keys of the stores of a principal**: at its unlock a named principal gives the storage service a storage key that its unlock key gives, through the root service, which keeps no copy; the storage service finds, makes and lists the stores of that principal only with it, and erases it at the lock, so a locked principal has no store that the service serves. The blocks are not encrypted yet.
- **Only a wide grant opens its act spaces.** The export `space_open` refuses the act spaces of a wide grant, even to the principal, with the kind `No_Grant`, so no caller can close the audit of a grant by opening it with another record size.
- **A surface of unlock in no capture, and the unlock hints.** Screen captures now mask every window of `unlock` and `unlock` accepts local keyboard input only, while format 3 principal files (`PRINCIPL` and `PRINCIPT`) store authenticated keyboard-layout, assistive, and language unlock hints under the seal AAD.
- **No memory object writable and executable at once.** The kernel tracks live writable, executable, and guest-switching mappings per memory object across `Memory_Map`, `Memory_Protect`, `Memory_Unmap`, guest memory, and process teardown, refusing any mapping or protection that would make an object writable and executable at once or map guest switching memory executable in any process.
- **No clock in a builder.** Sandboxed builder call hosts run with `Process_Flag.No_Clock` (heritable to child processes), omitting the time page mapping, trapping `rdtsc` and `rdtscp` with `#GP` through `CR4.TSD`, zeroing kernel entry return time, and disabling wall-clock deadlines so a builder cannot embed build time in its output.

## [0.97.0] - 2026-11-22 21:14

### Added

- **A module declares its record outputs and record inputs**: a package variable `requests: system.Record_Output(Access_Record)`, `orders: system.Record_Input(Order, .Many)` or `source: system.Record_Input_With_Terms(system.Any_Record, Condition)` becomes a row of the new tables `Record_Outputs` and `Record_Inputs` of the module file (format version 4), with its record description, its line of help, its pairing or its count of wirings, and its wiring terms; `system.Any_Record` declares a generic one. The build refuses a declaration that is not a package variable of the module, a record with padding or a Handle field, two streams with one name, and a generic record output with no generic record input. The contract package `output` gives the compartments of an output ring and the border that checks each one; no process writes a record output yet.
- **The module file writer.** The contract package `modus:contract/module_file_writer` writes a module file from its parts into memory that the caller gives, and refuses a part that a loader or a reader would refuse with a record that names the part; the tool writes each module through it with the same bytes as before, and a program of modus can write a module with the same code.
- **The wiring ring and the type check of a wiring**: the contract `output` describes the ring on which the root service gives a process each end of a wiring, one command for each end with its output ring, its index, its pace or its wiring terms, with the border that refuses each word that names nothing and the completion of the process, and it compares the record descriptions of the two ends of a wiring field by field. The context of a process grows from 16 to 18 handles: the handle 16 is the wiring ring of a process whose module declares a record output or a record input.
- **The command `Wire` and the wiring list of `Start`**: a process of the console or the start policy can ask the root service to join a record output of a process that it started, or of a service for which it acts for the principal, to a record input of another process or to itself; a `Start` from the console can carry the wiring of its child, which the root service applies whole before the first instruction of the child, or refuses whole. The asker writes its list in a list page that the root service owns, so a revocation by the asker cannot fault the root service. The root service checks that the two record descriptions are equal, pays each ring from the budget of the asker within limits for each asker, each process and the machine, writes the end of each wiring in an end page that the two sides read and cannot write, and records the wirings as the grant `wirings`, which `revoke` ends. Two records whose one field differs by its sign are no longer equal.
- **A process writes and reads its record streams**: `system.output_write` writes one record into the ring of each reader, whole or refused, `system.output_end` ends the stream, and `system.input_read` gives the next record, the joined record, a gap record or the end, with the end word of a writer that stopped; each reader checks each record against a description file that the root service gives it. A side refuses a ring whose counters no honest side writes and closes it, and a process that closes an end gives it back to the count of the root service, while the other side reads the end word `Side_Closed`. A module declares at most 64 record outputs and 64 record inputs. The module format is version 5: each record output and record input names the place of its variable, where each loader writes the index of its row. The machine test `output_wiring` checks each refusal of a wiring with the budget of its asker unchanged, the joined record at 0 and at 5, the end words of a writer, a reader and an asker that stop and of a revocation, and an end refused with `Busy` for a wiring ring that its side never reads.
- **An assembler module.** The module `assemble` reads a subset of x86-64 assembly in the syntax of AT&T, with an `export` directive that gives the types of each export, and writes a module file into a memory object that its caller lends; a text that it does not assemble whole is refused with its line and its column. The console command `isolate_memory(name, memory, size)` isolates the module that such an object holds, so `add(2, 3)` of a module assembled in the console gives 5.
- **The margin of the handle table of the root service**: the root service writes its handles and its mappings after the stop of each process that asks for wirings, and the measure record shows the count after the stop of the asker of the main test, so each run shows how close the table of 128 handles is to full. A start that a full table refuses now names the table in its line.
- **The editor `edit`.** `start("edit")` opens a window that edits one text of its private store, with no modes and no save action: a character goes in at the caret, Shift with the arrows, Home, End or the pages selects, the pointer places the caret and selects with a drag, a long line goes on more rows, and a gutter numbers the lines. Each word, line end, or change before a move to another line is one commit, so Ctrl+Z restores the version before from the store, also after a restart, and Ctrl+Y goes forward again. A text holds at most 32768 bytes, and an invalid sequence from the store shows as the replacement character. The clipboard, Ctrl+F and the edit of another object are not built yet.
- **A C compiler and a thin libc.** The module `c_compiler` is `chibicc`, ported: `compile(source, output)` writes a text of assembly that the module `assemble` takes, with an `export` for each function that is not `static`, over a thin libc with no path and no standard stream. In the console, `int add(int a, int b) { return a + b; }` is compiled, assembled, isolated, and `add(2, 3)` gives 5. The assembler takes `movsxd`; the rest of what `chibicc` emits past `+` and `-` is refused with its line and its column.
- **The measures of the log facts.** In a measure run, `log_reader` prints the time of `system.log` and of `system.log_fact` for the same line, of the operation `Log` for each form, and of the render of a fact for 30 rows of the window of the log. After the main machine, the tool prints the log records up to the start of the desktop, the bytes of the module files of the root service and of the compositor, and the records that the lines would take in a record of 128 bytes; the measure record keeps the first two. `modustool measure-compare <record> <record>` prints each measure of two measure records and the ratio of each number. No test asserts these numbers.
- **A merge queue by batches.** `modustool queue <branch>...` merges a batch of branches into `master` after one full check of the whole batch, each branch with its own merge commit, then pushes `master` and removes the merged branches and their worktrees. When the check fails, it checks the halves of the batch to name the branch at fault and merges the halves that pass: one branch at fault costs at most 1 + 2⌈log₂ n⌉ full checks, 9 for 12 branches, and a batch never passes its budget of min(2n − 1, 1 + 4⌈log₂ n⌉) full checks.
- **Record streams in the console**: a line names a record output or a record input of a process by its typed name, `output_counter.counts`, and reads a record output as a value of the kind `Stream`: `where` checks a condition on each record, and its first condition goes with the wiring so the writer writes only the records that pass it, and the console refuses a record that fails it as a fault of the writer; `columns`, `first(s, n)` and `table(s)` work on it, `stream_counts(s)` gives the counts that the writer published, and a `Stream` at the end of a line draws each row as it comes, with its joined record and its gaps, until its end or Escape. `wire(from, to)` gives a row of a wiring list, `+` joins two lists, and `start(module, list)` starts a module with its wirings made before its first instruction. `record_outputs(module)` lists the record outputs of a module.
- **The scale factor of the reference screen.** The contract package `scale_factor` holds the scale of the desktop in eighths, from 1.25 to 2, with one border that refuses each other count and refuses a scale past 1.375 until every window fits its logical screen; the execution of a draw table draws each kind of record at a scale, with each edge rounded to the nearest pixel of the screen, so two boxes that share an edge meet with no gap. No window draws at a scale yet: the desktop at 1.25 is the next step.
- **The rename of the library as a command.** `modustool rename-library` moves the folders of the source library into its two source libraries with `git mv`, rewrites the import lines, the comments and the documents, and names each string of a source that still holds an old form. A second run changes nothing, so each branch runs it after its merge of `master`, and the rename waits for no branch.
- **The counted kernel.** `modustool fuzz operations` now runs on the test kernel `kernel_edge_counters`, which counts the edges of the kernel code that each operation sequence reaches, for the runner and its children only, on one processor, in counters that only the tool reads through the memory file of the machine; the tool keeps each sequence whose two runs agree on a new edge class, mutates the kept ones, writes the smallest set of them as the reach inputs of the corpus at the end of the run, and checks in each run that the counters stay at 0 outside the sequences and past the guards of the linked kernel. The release refuses that kernel by the hash that its description must name, by the test signing key of its volume, and by the text `kernel: TEST BUILD:` in the bytes of a kernel module, which refuses each test kernel; each build checks that the kernel that ships lacks that text and that each test kernel holds it, and a machine test of another volume fails when its log has the boot line of the counted kernel. The hashes of `kernel` and `kernel_without_return_check` change only through the line numbers in the messages of their bounds checks.
- **The whole log of a process, kept, and the whole log as it comes**: a starter wires the record output `log` of its child in the list of its start, and the reader then receives each log record of the child from the first one, while the kernel log still refuses what passes its budget of each second; a reader that acts for the principal wires `log.follow` and receives each record of the whole log as it comes, with one gap record where the kernel wrote over records before the log service passed them on.
- **A failed wait says what the host gave the machine, and the parts at once fit in the memory of the host**: the line of a step of a machine test that ends at its bound gives the processor time that the host gave the machine over the wait, the time that its threads waited for a processor and the memory stall of the host, and ends with "the host starved the machine", "the machine was idle" or "the machine had the processor"; the processors report gives the run state of QEMU. `modustool test` and `modustool screenshots` run at most as many parts at once as the available memory of the host holds, less 4 GiB, at 1 GiB for each part, besides the bound by processors, so the host no longer swaps the memory of a machine.
- **The kinds of a log fact for every line**: a fact type can have no field, and its sentence is its template; a fact type can be a distinct type of a shared record, as `errors.Error_Record_Fact_Fields`, the seven values of an error record with the kind and the unit by their names. A field writes 16 hexadecimal digits (`Hexadecimal_Sixteen_Digits`), the head of a hash (`Hash_Head`, `1a2b3c4d…`), hundredths (`1.05`), nanoseconds as microseconds (`812.4 us`), and a status of `operation.Status`, `errors.Kind` or `errors.Unit` by the name of its member, also in a word type of 8 bytes (`operation.Status_Word`). A template has up to 512 bytes, and each reader of a log file in the tool reads the sentences of the facts.
- **Text views and facts of several records**: a log fact holds texts of any length up to 1 KiB as text views of 16 bytes (`value_kinds.Text_View`), each with its length and its first bytes in the record and the rest in the out-of-line area of the fact, which takes up to 9 records in a row and one line on the serial port; a reader that loses a record of a fact shows its records raw with a mark, never a wrong sentence, and `where` compares a view by its length and its start first. A load error is a fact of one shape for each stage (`module.Load_Find_Fields` and the others). New field kinds write tenths (`12.3`), an address with no leading zero (`0x1a2b`) and a time of day (`07:04`), and a status of `operation.Object_Kind`, `module.Load_Stage`, `module.Error`, `module.Difference`, `block.Status`, `storage_status.Status`, `call_status.Call_Status` and the problems of a pointer theme is written by the name of its member, each also in a word type of 8 bytes; `storage.Status` and `system.Call_Status` moved into the contract packages `storage_status` and `call_status`.
- **Utility modules: merge and filter**: `merge` joins several record streams of one record into one, and `filter` keeps the records of a stream that pass one condition; each works on a stream of any record, whose record the first wiring fixes and the root service checks for each later source. In the console, `start("merge", wire(a, merge.sources) + wire(b, merge.sources))` then `merge.merged` reads the joined stream.
- **A machine for a viewer on the host**: `modustool run --remote-display <viewer key>` runs the machine with no window, starts `remote_display`, prints the port of the UDP relay of the peer to its port 7101 and the public key of the service, and allows the viewer key for the view and the input. The native viewer then connects with `viewertool machine <port> <key>`. The machine stops when the viewer has ended. The UDP relay of the peer now reaches any port of the machine that its start names.
- **Record literals, named arguments, `let`, `with` and the placeholder `_` in the console**: a line writes a record by its fields, `area({x = 0, y = 0, width = 11, height = 3})`, typed by the parameter that receives it; gives a parameter by its name, `grow(r, by = 2)`; changes some fields of a record, `r with {width = 10}`; names values for one expression, `let s = {…} in area(s) + area(s)`; and puts the value on the left of `|>` in another place, `3 |> grow(r, _)`. An unknown field, a missing field, an unknown or missing parameter and a `_` out of its place are refused with the name and the place. `let`, `in`, `with` and `_` are no longer names.
- **Record outputs and record inputs of a module**: a start list holds, after its start rows, wiring rows that join a record output of a row to a record input of an earlier row, and the default start policy gives them with the start of the writer, so the reader reads every record from the serial 0. The tool refuses at the build a row whose reader starts after its writer, a row that loops, an end that a start policy does not hold, a writer row with restarts, and a stream that the module files of the two rows do not declare or whose records differ; the root service takes from a start policy no other row, so a start policy from the disk reads no stream of its children. The default start list wires the demonstration pair `wired_writer` and `wired_reader`.
- **One test part from a range of its steps.** `modustool test main steps 1270 to 1280` boots the machine, runs its boot steps, the setup steps that each `with <first> to <last>` names and the steps of the range, then ends the part, with the time of each as a measure; `of <log name>` names another machine of the part. A range that names no step, ends before it starts or passes the steps of the machine is refused, and so is a step that needs a mark or the key of `remote` that no selected step gives, with the step to add.
- **A check of the waits of the scenarios before any machine runs.** After its build, `modustool check without_machines` and `modustool test` hold each text of the log that a step waits for, and that starts with the prefix of a fact template, against the fact templates of the module files and the text lines of the system; a fact whose sentence changed, a fact split into two or a list that became one fact for each row fails there with its file, its line and the nearest template, not at the hang bound of a machine. `modustool fact_waits` runs it alone; the 1879 fact waits of the scenarios pass it.
- **A full check that names every failed part.** `modustool screenshots` and the workflows build the check of the flake with `--keep-going`, so every test part runs past the first one that fails, and `modustool failed_parts` lists the host checks and each part that failed or did not run, with its first failed step, its machine and its whole build log in `build/failed_parts/`.
- **The display text of a member, apart from its name**: a module file (version 7) holds a table of display texts by member and language tag of BCP 47 (`en`, `fr-CA`), which a module declares in its source apart from the enum: `@(member_texts = "en")`. A reader follows the tags of the principal as the Lookup of RFC 4647 does (`fr-CA`, `fr`), then `en`, then the name of the member. The palette shows "settings.set scale to 1.75" and the button "1.75" for the scale `.fourteen_eighths`, the completion lists `.fourteen_eighths   1.75`, and a line still types the name. The settings give texts for the scale, the subpixel order (`RGB`, `BGR`), the pointer theme and the keyboard layout (`US`). A row that names no member, a text past 24 bytes, a text with a character that a reader does not see or that changes the order of the text, a text of spaces only, a tag that is not well-formed or not in its canonical case, and two texts for one member and one tag are refused, and the build refuses a row of the palette that would miss a button.
- **Functions, lists and `map` in the console**: a line writes a function, `(x) => x * 2`, names it with `let` and calls it, `let double = (x) => x * 2 in double(21)`; writes a list, `[1, 2, 3]`; and calls a function on each item, `map([1, 2, 3], (x) => x * 2)` gives `[2, 4, 6]`. A function reads the bindings of its line where it is written, and its body runs only in a call. A wrong count of arguments, a call of a value that is no function, a parameter named twice, and a function or a list given to a command or kept by a variable are refused with their place. A line that nests deeper than 512 KiB of stack of the evaluator, as a recursion, stops with an error: the console runs its lines on a thread of 640 KiB of stack, where the deepest lines of 200 bytes took more than its 64 KiB before.
- **Comments in Lex**: `//` outside a text starts a comment to the end of the line, `area(r) // the area of r`. The console draws it in the color of a comment and runs the line before it. A line of only a comment, or of only spaces, now does nothing, where a line of spaces said that the line ends too soon. `//` inside a text stays a part of the text, and `a / / b` stays an error.
- **Each angle is in turns.** The package `modus:support/trigonometry` gives the sine and the cosine of an angle in turns (1 is a whole circle, 0.25 a right angle), exact at each whole quarter turn, and each angle of the system, its guides and its site now counts in turns. `tone` and the sine table of `plasma` use it in place of a series in radians and an approximation of Bhaskara.
- **Vector fuzzy matching and frecency ranking.** Console completion and the desktop palette now rank matches with a bounded frecency bonus (0 to 8 points, use-counted decay of 1/32, capacity 64, rebuilt from the line history in the console and kept in compositor memory for the palette) and match ASCII names with a portable 128-bit vector form verified score-for-score against the scalar reference. Measured on the host (`-o:speed -microarch:x86-64`, load 6.90), the vector form matches 51.9 to 60.6 million names a second over 100 names (6.0x to 7.2x scalar), 32.4 to 66.2 million over 10 000 names (4.8x to 8.2x), and 34.3 to 64.9 million over 100 000 names (4.7x to 7.5x).
- **Swiss tables and rapidhash V3 for in-memory lookups.** The support packages `modus:support/hash_table` (`Hash_Table` and `Hash_Index`, 16-slot groups with SSE2 tag matching, 7/8 load bound, in-place tombstone rebuild, and seeded hashing) and `modus:support/fast_hash` (rapidhash V3, keeping BLAKE3 for identity and security) give modules fixed-capacity, zero-allocation tables for outside keys. On the host (`-o:speed -microarch:x86-64`, load 3.71), random-key lookups measure 1.46 ns hit / 1.29 ns miss at 16 entries, 2.21 / 1.82 ns at 1 000 entries, and 4.11 / 5.88 ns at 100 000 entries, and rapidhash V3 hashes 8 B and 16 B keys in 2.68 ns, 32 B in 3.71 ns, and 64 B in 6.19 ns. The private glyph cache (`text_rendering.Private_Cache`) now uses `hash_table.Hash_Index` in place of its chained bucket table, keeping the same 354-page glyph memory budget.
- **The page Credits and licences.** `docs/CREDITS.md` now unifies reused code, reimplemented algorithms, design inspirations, and upstream funding links in one tool-checked document, rendered on the site at `docs/reference/licenses/` and linked from the footer of every page.
- **Role contracts, root role registry, and Display conformance suite**: added the `role_contract` contract package with the closed `Role` enum, deterministic BLAKE3 contract hashing, `module_fills_contract` verification, and the `Display` role contract; added the root service role registry and pure `role_policy` enforcing per-scope single holders, trusted-path principal 0 rules, and caller-scoped local service bindings; and added the holder-agnostic `display_conformance` suite with host tests.
- **Immutable trees with a domain-separated tree hash and mark-and-sweep collection of `module_files`.** The storage service freezes mutable subtrees bottom-up into canonical immutable tree objects named by a domain-separated BLAKE3 tree hash (`freeze_tree`, `tree_read`, `tree_entry`), and collects unreachable content-addressed objects in `module_files` (`collect_garbage`) while keeping every hash reachable from signed system modules, kept applied plans, disk grant modules and pinned trees, refusing corrupt or over-deep graphs with zero deletions.
- **Kernel record and replay of one thread (layer 1).** Added the `input_history` contract package, the pure kernel part `replay_filter`, three `Thread_Control` commands (`Record_Start`, `Record_End`, `Replay_Start`) requiring the `Debug` right with no new kernel operation, kernel trapping and emulation of `rdtsc`/`rdtscp`/`cpuid` via `CR4.TSD` and CPUID faulting, point copies for read-only shared pages, single-processor pinning, one-stint bound and immediate revocation end, the `replay_probe` test module, and a tool rule check refusing `rdrand` and `rdseed` outside `random.entropy_gather`.
- **The clipboard, the search and any object in the editor `edit`**: the clipboard carries multi-line UTF-8 text (LF line ends and tabs) and Control+X cut while single-line fields keep refusing line ends; `edit` adds Ctrl+A, Ctrl+C, Ctrl+X, Ctrl+V, incremental Ctrl+F search with match highlights and wrap-around navigation, and `edit(store, name)` in the console to edit any text object of a store of the principal.
- **Supervision contracts, restart window, and client call retry rules**: added the `supervision` contract package with explicit-value enums and transport borders, the fixed-layout `Connection_Page` with atomic seqlock transitions across holder restarts, the `Request_Serial_Log` deduplication table for at-most-once execution, and the single-retry `Client_Call_Tracker` state machine; added the pure `Supervision_Tree` with restart strategies, optional sliding restart window, dependency cycle refusal, and bounded escalation to `start_policy_rules`, with host tests.
- **Declarations in the console.** Lex adds `blake3:` hash literals, multi-line `.lex` files, `if ... then ... else ...`, comparisons, `filter`, call on a record literal (`plan { ... }`, `project { ... }`), and a pure lazy evaluator that runs in the zero-authority module `declaration_eval` with cycle detection, step and depth bounds, and deterministic canonical output.
- **Powerbox Layer 1 consent contract, policy engine, and service**: added the `powerbox` contract package with closed enums, 16-item grouped consent batches, sealed trusted display dialogs, and strict border validators; added `Role.Powerbox` and `powerbox_contract()` to `role_contract`; added the pure `powerbox_core` policy state machine enforcing window ownership, 3-dialog per-session queue caps, 250 ms anti-clickjacking arm delay, 500 ms rate limiting, 60 s expiry, hash-bound `Always` grants for disk modules only, isolated store pickers, and whole-or-nothing plan difference dialogs; and added the freestanding `powerbox` service module.
- **Work budget contract and per-connection work thread probe**: added the `work_budget` contract package with closed `Work_Account_State` and `Work_Budget_Refusal` enums, `Work_Budget_Requirements`, border-validated `Accounting_Snapshot` records, `work_budget_check`, and the `Connection_Worker_Table` state machine isolating client exhaustion and revocation; added the `work_accounting_probe` test module spawning one work thread and scratch memory per connection strictly on the client's offered child budget with `Spend` right, with adversarial host tests.
- **Language-neutral module ABI and hardened ELF converter**: the build tool now converts freestanding System V AMD64 relocatable ELF objects (`ET_REL`) paired with a plain-text `Module_Manifest` directly into validated modus module files without Odin AST scraping or dynamic linking, and hardens ELF header, string table, symbol, and relocation checks across both object and linked converters.
- **The C of the C compiler past addition.** The assembler now encodes 8- and 16-bit registers and moves (with REX handling for `%spl`/`%bpl`/`%sil`/`%dil` versus `%ah`/`%ch`/`%dh`/`%bh`), scaled indexed SIB addressing, bitwise and shift instructions (`and`, `or`, `xor`, `not`, `shl`/`sal`, `shr`, `sar`), `test`, `imul`, `idiv`, `div`, `neg`, `inc`, `dec`, all 16 `setcc` conditions, sign- and zero-extending moves (`movsbl`/`movzbl`/`movswl`/`movzwl`/`movslq`/`movzb`/`movzx` and kin), zero-operand sign extensions (`cbw`, `cwtl`, `cltq`, `cwd`, `cdq`, `cqo`), `rep stosb`, and indirect `call`/`jmp`. C functions compiled by `c_compiler` with loops, comparisons, recursion, multiplication, division, bitwise operations, local initializers, and byte/short arrays now assemble into valid modules and run on modus.
- **Object cache wire protocol, signed realisations, substitution policy, and host cache server.** Added the freestanding contract package `modus:contract/object_cache` with strict binary framing, domain-separated Ed25519 signing payloads for realisations and uploads, and pure `substitution_check` verification (hash-only trust for blobs and canonical trees, explicit trusted-signer verification for realisations), together with the content-addressed host server and `modustool cache-serve` command in `tools/modustool/cache_server`.
- **Semantic tree records in the draw table.** Version 20 of the draw record adds `Element_Push` and `Element_Pop` with the WAI-ARIA 1.2 element roles, states, live modes, and 16-byte detail records validated by the unified clip-and-element order border (`damage_of_draw_table_20` and `execute_draw_table_in_rectangles_20`). The `accessibility` contract package reconstructs, audits, and diffs flat semantic trees directly from a draw table, and the interface widgets (`button`, `text_input`, `text_input_line`, `list_with_choice`, `list_of_rows`, `scroll_bar`, `action_bar`, `card`, `draw_heading`, `bar_with_value`, and `case_lines`) emit semantic elements in the same draw table that draws their pixels.
- **Collector hash-chained act verifier and Wide Grants Layer 1**: added the `wide_grants` contract package with closed enums, bounded window-scoped `Wide_Grant_Terms`, passphrase and host-key principal proofs, domain-separated BLAKE3 `"modus act record v1"` hash-chained `Act_Record` validation and chain verification, and sensitive-action principal-proof guards; added the pure `collector_core` append-only act collector and fail-closed `Wide_Grant_Manager`; and added the freestanding `collector` system service module.
- **Interface messages contract, MessageFormat 2 evaluator, and MODLANG1 binary language packs**: added the freestanding `interface_messages` contract package with canonical BCP 47 language preference lists (at most 8 tags) and RFC 4647 Lookup resolution, a zero-allocation W3C MessageFormat 2 parser and evaluator supporting `.input`, `.local`, `.match` selectors with CLDR cardinal plural rules and `:integer`/`:number`/`:string` functions, and the BLAKE3-verified `MODLANG1` binary language pack format with O(log N) sorted lookup and adversarial host tests.
- **Zero-allocation LZ4 block codec, Binary Fuse8 filter, and commit-driven bigram store search index.** Three freestanding packages join `modus:contract`: `lz4` (up to 5.8 GiB/s compress and 12.2 GiB/s decompress on repetitive 64 KiB text), `xor_filter` (9.5 bits per key at 100 000 keys, 0% false negatives, 2.5–3.4 ns lookup), and `store_search` (103x–189x faster than brute-force search across 200 files, with O(1) subtree index reuse by `tree_hash`).
- **Declarative plans, recipes, and projects contracts and services**: added the `plans_recipes` contract package with canonical plan hashing, grant-difference computation feeding `powerbox` (`Show_Plan_Difference`), `applied_plans` store records and rollback validation, domain-separated `recipe_hash` and `Substitution_Policy` cache verification, deterministic `pins.lex` formatting and strict parsing, unified source resolver records, and `projects.enter`/`exit` console specifications; added the pure `plans_core`, `recipes_core`, and `projects_core` state machines with adversarial host tests; and added the freestanding `plans`, `recipes`, and `projects` system service modules.
