- **Machine mesh contract, CPaceRistretto255 PAKE, signed cross-principal grants, and store delta sync.** Added the freestanding contract package `modus:contract/machine_mesh` with monotonic offline-root-signed machine lists (per-machine keys, instant revocation without changing principal identity, and default non-TPM join refusal with opt-in warning), off-by-default keyed BLAKE3 local address hints, signed cross-principal grants bounded by a one-day lifetime or monotonic revocation lists, CPace PAKE over RFC 9496 Ristretto255 with mutual key confirmation, and the store delta sync wire protocol with three-way fast-forward and conflict-fork reconciliation.
- **An absolute pointer in a virtual machine.** The input service drives `virtio-tablet-pci` alongside the PS/2 fallback, batches `ABS_X`, `ABS_Y`, button, and wheel updates until `SYN_REPORT` so the pointer never steps along one axis before the other, and scales axis ranges onto the screen with exact integer arithmetic clamped at the edges.
- **The pointer of a viewer and the echo of its input.** Remote display protocol version 2 adds `Pointer_Shape`, `Pointer_Picture`, and `Pointer_Warp` messages together with a monotonic 64-bit `input_echo_count` on every `Viewer_Frame_Start` and `Pointer_Warp`, so a viewer renders its own local cursor with zero wait and measures exact input-to-display latency.
- **The uses of a viewer key across a restart.** The disk grant table (format 3, 1168 bytes) persists each client key's `Calls`, `View`, and `Input` uses alongside its Ed25519 key under the BLAKE2b tag, so an allowed viewer reconnects with its screen and input permissions after a machine restart until revoked.
- **SDK packager, verifier, and language-neutral module converter**: `modustool sdk-pack`, `modustool sdk-verify`, and `modustool sdk-convert` assemble and cryptographically verify a self-contained SDK directory with BLAKE3 per-file and root digests plus Ed25519 signatures, and build outside modules in Odin or any compiled language emitting freestanding `ET_REL` objects with no repository checkout and no access to `internal:` or `modulus:`.
- **Sandboxed guest builder contract and freestanding module.** Added the freestanding contract package `modus:contract/guest_builder` with fixed-layout `Guest_Build_Spec`, closed border enums, canonical `MODGBD1` disk image packing and recursive `tree_hash` output extraction, and the pure `Guest_Sandbox_Runner` enforcing read-only input disks, zero network, frozen epoch time, and exit-code-0 output extraction, together with the freestanding `guest_builder` system module wired to `Guest_Run`.
- **The guide of Lex, rich and whole**: added `docs/LEX.md` and linked it from `README.md` and the site, covering both evaluation modes, the full grammar and value kinds, integers with units, pipes and `_`, list and table transforms, plans, recipes, projects and `pins.lex`, record stream short forms and the body region, ASCII evaluation diagrams, all error refusal kinds with fixes, and a frequent tasks reference table.
- **Body region contract and record stream short-form expansion**: added the fixed-layout `Body_Region_Header` and `Body_Reference` ring-buffer contract (`body_region_write`, `body_region_read`) in `modus:contract/output` with border validation for bounds, alignment, wrap generations, zero length, and serials; added pure wiring-list expansion and validation for `audit`, `audited`, `keep`, `merge`, and `collector.verify` in `internal:console/console_stream`; and refused `collector` service calls in mode `declaration`.
- **Data evolution contract package (`data_evolution`).** A freestanding, zero-allocation contract package provides canonical stored type descriptions with reader-computed domain-separated BLAKE3 description hashes, the `MODTYPE1` self-describing typed object format with strict border validation, derived forward and backward migration planning and fail-closed execution, stated-loss accounting, and pre-flight rollback checks for 1.0 store evolution without forcing pre-1.0 stores into migrations yet.
- **Host agent bridge contract and stdio JSON-RPC adapter.** Added the freestanding contract package `modus:contract/agent_bridge` with canonical `"MODAB1"` binary framing, closed border enums, Lex sensitive-action detection, and pure authorization rules that require explicit principal approval for grant-adding plans, together with the `modustool agent-bridge` stdio JSON-RPC 2.0 adapter, remote QUIC call transport wrapper, and append-only BLAKE3 act log enforcement.
- **Browser machine software MMU and x86-64 user-mode processor.** Added the freestanding contract package `modus:contract/software_machine` with a 4-level x86-64 software MMU matching the page-table format of `modulus` (including 2 MiB large pages, canonical checks, W^X/NX enforcement, atomic page-straddling memory accesses, and a software TLB), a user-mode x86-64 interpreter with `gs:` base support, `syscall` trapping, strict step budgets, and core SSE2 operations, and the browser machine configuration border supporting single-worker fallback without cross-origin isolation and optional bring-your-own link relay URLs.
- **VirtIO-GPU 2D scanout, Venus framing, and WebGPU subset contracts.** Added the freestanding `virtio_gpu` and `webgpu_subset` contracts (including strict precompiled SPIR-V validation and binary GPU command stream checks) and the `gpu_device` PCI device service for VirtIO-GPU 2D scanout setup and Venus capability probing.
- **Self-hosting verification command and bootstrap recipe chain harness.** `modustool self-host` compiles `tools/modustool` in two isolated staging directories where Stage 1 compiles Stage 2 with identical deterministic flags, proving exact BLAKE3 fixed-point equality or reporting the first diverging byte offset, and `bootstrap_chain_verify` validates the stage-by-stage `chibicc` self-host, `hex0`-to-`odin` guest bootstrap, and SQLite, Lua, and zstd C-world recipe DAGs.
- **Root delegation contracts, services, and 14-role BLAKE3 catalog.** Added border validators for `Start_From_Bytes`, `Disk_Rows_Set`, and PCI device identity/grant specifications, the delegated `disk_processes` and `devices` services, the pure daytime appearance rule in `settings`, and canonical BLAKE3 role contracts for all 14 remaining core roles.
- **Venus Vulkan wire protocol, draw-table WebGPU translator, and processor 3D rasterizer.** Added the freestanding `venus_vulkan` package and its `webgpu_stream_to_venus` translator for `virtio_gpu.Submit_3D`, plus `draw_table_translate_to_webgpu` and the `software_3d_execute` processor rasterizer in `webgpu_subset` with 24-bit depth testing, scissor clipping, and linear-light alpha blending.
- **Text grid benchmark against text runs.** `interface` now includes a pixel-for-pixel verification and opt-in `-define:MEASURE=true` benchmark comparing `Text_Grid` against equivalent `Text` and `Rectangle` runs across 104 by 32 terminal frames and a full 1 920 by 1 080 terminal (240 by 67 cells). In the 104 by 32 worst case (one color and fill per cell), runs require 6 656 records (past the 4 096-record bound) and 322 816 bytes against 1 record and 53 296 bytes for `Text_Grid`, so `Text_Grid` stays its own draw record kind.
- **Stall-deadline revocation for full holding rings.** The `wiring` service now tracks monotonic stall deadlines (`wiring_stall_observe`, `wiring_stall_expire`) for active `Always_Holds_Writer` wirings whose output ring stays full without reader `read`-cursor progress, resetting the deadline immediately when the reader advances. Once a ring remains full for `STALL_HANG_BOUND_MS` (60 000 ms), `wiring` revokes only that stalled wiring, writes `End_Word.Revoked` and wakes its end word so the blocked writer unblocks immediately, and leaves healthy sibling wirings and `Never_Holds_Writer` wirings intact.
- **Host test seam for the `system.output_write` ring loop.** A host-only seam seeds full rings at and past their largest gap count so host tests run `system.output_write` itself: stalled rings close with a `Closed` completion, `rings_past_gap` increments, and sibling healthy rings still receive the exact record.
- **Current and peak handle and mapping counts in `Process_Report`.** The kernel tracks current and peak handle and mapping counts per process and reports `handle_count`, `handles_peak`, `mapping_count`, and `mappings_peak` in `Inspect(.Process)` and `monitor.processes()`, allowing the root service to read its table counts in one inspection call.
- **Optional typed error record on failed or refused process stops.** `Process_Stop` accepts a readable `errors.Error_Record` address for a `Failed` or `Refused` self-stop, stores it in `Stop_Report.error`, and refuses any non-zero error address on `Done`, `Stopped_On_Request`, or holder stops; `system.stop` passes the optional record and the console prints it on call-host failure.
- **Pure engines for replay shared-writer gating and debugger reverse checkpoints.** `replay_filter` adds the `Shared_Writer_Gate` state machine enforcing input-point gating, the one-stint wait bound, and immediate revocation override; `input_history` adds `Checkpoint_Ring`, `step_back_plan`, and `watch_back_plan` for reverse stepping and reverse data watchpoints.
- **Multi-stream sound mixer and C11 SDK headers.** Added the freestanding `sound_mixer` contract package for 8-stream saturating 48 kHz stereo PCM mixing, mandatory microphone capture indicator tracking, and integer device clock drift estimation, and added `modustool c-headers` to generate C11 contract headers with `_Static_assert` size and offset checks derived from Odin layouts.
- **Palette selection and clipboard.** The palette search bar and argument form fields support keyboard text selection (Shift+Left, Shift+Right, Shift+Home, Shift+End, Control+A) with visible selection highlighting, and copy (Control+C), cut (Control+X), and paste (Control+V) through the compositor clipboard.
- **The list of the viewers from the mark.** Pressing the remote display viewer mark in the bar opens a compact popover listing each connected viewer with its key prefix, grant role (`view` or `input`), and an explicit `disconnect` button that ends that viewer's connection through `Capture_Disconnect` without opening the console.
- **Resizable terminal grid core and headless memory screen contract.** Added the freestanding terminal grid resize package (`internal:terminal/grid_resize`) wired into the terminal service with `terminal.Input.Resized` notifications, and the freestanding headless virtual framebuffer contract (`modus:contract/memory_screen`) with strict geometry, stride, damage, and atomic viewer-resize validation.
- **Project console session lifecycle**: `projects.enter` and `exit()` manage bounded, nested child project consoles per principal without modifying the applied plan or installing modules globally, requiring verified trusted display consent when entering adds module or store grants outside the current session and revoking all temporary connections and store shares on exit.
- **Console `log` wiring and `log.follow` loop breaker**: the console supports naming a module's implicit `log` output in `wire(server.log, collector.sources)` and piping `log.follow |> where(level = Warning)`, protected by a self-feedback loop breaker that suppresses render echoing into the kernel log and excludes the console's own process slot by default.
- **The other records at the addresses of the kernel operations.** The kernel operation fuzzer now writes typed valid, boundary, and invalid records for `Event_Rule`, `Guest_Registers`, `Thread_Schedule`, `Process_Schedule`, `Thread_Placement`, and `Address_Range` tables, verified by deterministic host tests against each contract border.
- **Browser machine WebAssembly runtime exports and runner page**: the software machine exposes a freestanding, zero-allocation C-ABI WebAssembly export surface (`modus_vm_init`, `modus_vm_load_bytes`, `modus_vm_step_budget`, `modus_vm_framebuffer_ptr`, `modus_vm_framebuffer_bytes`, `modus_vm_push_key`, `modus_vm_push_pointer`, `modus_vm_pop_serial`, and `modus_vm_state_word`) that builds cleanly for `freestanding_wasm32`, `freestanding_wasm64p32`, and `freestanding_amd64_sysv`, paired with a self-contained browser runner asset (`browser_machine.html`) supporting single-processor execution without cross-origin isolation and an optional bring-your-own link relay URL field.
- **Canonical bootstrap and C-world recipe catalog with native x86-64 compilation proofs**: added typed reproducible `plans_recipes.Recipe_Spec` and `guest_builder.Guest_Build_Spec` catalog definitions for `chibicc_stage1`/`chibicc_stage2`, `sqlite`, `lua`, `zstd`, and the 6-stage `live_bootstrap_chain` (`hex0` to `odin`), verified alongside deterministic bit-for-bit BLAKE3 tests of the native x86-64 assembler and C + assembly `ET_REL` to `.modus` conversion.
- **A recovery key for a named principal on a machine with no TPM.** Named principals sealed under a passphrase alone can now seal and open an authenticated recovery-key copy bound to their principal number and public keys, allowing recovery after a lost passphrase on machines without a TPM or with Secure Boot off.
- **Accessibility semantic checker, forced high-contrast colors, and speech queue contract.** Added direct draw-table semantic auditing and role/name/state widget querying in `modus:contract/accessibility`, WCAG AAA forced high-contrast color token palettes with pure integer luminance checks in `modus:graphics`, and the freestanding `modus:contract/speech_output` announcement queue and element utterance formatter bridged to `sound_mixer`.
- **Remote display agent settle and browser gateway token contracts.** Added freestanding agent capture and settle tracking (`Agent_Capture_Mode`, `Settle_Tracker`), signed 1-day browser `Display_Token` verification and renewal with an immutable absolute expiry ceiling and `Token_Revocation_List`, and shared WebTransport/WebSocket binary gateway framing in `modus:contract/remote_display`.
- **SSE2 vector spans for translucent blends, horizontal gradients, and path coverage.** Added bit-for-bit SSE2 vector forms for translucent solid span blending (`blend_span_simd`), horizontal gradients (`horizontal_gradient_span_simd`, `horizontal_gradient_with_alpha_span_simd`, `mix_with_alpha_span_simd`), and path coverage scanline accumulation and compositing (`path_coverage_span_simd`) in `modus:graphics`, wired into `blend_fill`, `gradient`, `gradient_with_alpha`, and draw-table path and gradient execution.
- **TLS 1.3 over TCP record layer and handshake library.** Added the freestanding zero-allocation `modus:tls_tcp` package implementing RFC 8446 record framing, SHA-256 HKDF key schedule, X25519 key exchange, Ed25519 `CertificateVerify`, and full client/server handshakes over `TLS_AES_128_GCM_SHA256` and `TLS_CHACHA20_POLY1305_SHA256` reusing `modus:cryptography_primitives`.
- **Freestanding PNG and baseline JPEG decoders with SSE2 vector forms.** Added zero-allocation `modus:contract/image_decode` supporting PNG (bit depth 8, color types 0, 2, 3, 4, and 6, CRC-32, PLTE/tRNS, concatenated IDAT zlib/DEFLATE stored, fixed, and dynamic Huffman, Adler-32, and SSE2 scanline filters) and baseline JPEG (DQT, DHT, SOF0 grayscale and YCbCr 4:4:4/4:2:2/4:2:0, DRI restart intervals, SOS byte stuffing, SSE2 integer 2D IDCT, and SSE2 YCbCr-to-RGBA conversion), with strict dimension/bomb bounds and zero pixel writes on refusal.
- **HTTP/1.1 client, HTTP/3 framing bridge, and per-principal web store.** Added the freestanding `modus:contract/http_client` package with strict HTTP/1.1 smuggling and chunked-decoding guards, RFC 9114 HTTP/3 `HEADERS`/`DATA` framing, and per-principal partitioned `Cookie_Jar` and `Response_Cache` enforcing `Secure`, `HttpOnly`, `SameSite`, `Domain`, `Path`, and `Cache-Control` without cross-principal reads or eviction.
- **WHATWG-subset HTML parser and CSS block, inline, and flex layout engine.** Added the freestanding `modus:contract/html_css` package with a fixed-pool iterative HTML tokenizer and origin-isolated DOM builder bounded at 512 nodes and depth 64, CSS specificity cascade and inheritance, block/inline/flex pixel layout, and canonical `draw_record` primitive emission.
- **TPM 2.0 salted session, remote quote verifier, and rollback floor contracts.** Added freestanding `modus:contract/tpm_attestation` with `KDFa`/HMAC-SHA-256 salted session parameter encryption (constant-time AES-128-CFB and XOR stream), strict `TPMS_ATTEST` quote verification over PCR 7, 11, and 12, and monotonic grant-table counters with `TPM2_PolicyAuthorize` floor enforcement.
- **NVMe 1.4+ queue pair and command protocol contract.** Added freestanding `modus:contract/nvme` defining exact 64-byte submission and 16-byte completion entries, closed opcode and status enums, `Queue_Pair_State` with phase-tag wraparound and in-flight command ID tracking, doorbell offset overflow guards, and validated Admin and I/O command builders.
- **Whole-store snapshot history and atomic rollback contract.** Added freestanding `modus:contract/store_history` providing a bounded immutable snapshot DAG with canonical `tree_hash` commits, historical object lookup, atomic whole-store rollback that preserves newer snapshots, and sorted snapshot diffs.
- **An SSH client.** Added the freestanding zero-allocation `modus:ssh_client` package and `ssh_client` library module implementing RFC 4253/4252/4254 SSH-2 transport (`curve25519-sha256`, `ssh-ed25519` with strict `Known_Hosts` verification and no silent TOFU, `chacha20-poly1305@openssh.com`), Ed25519 public-key authentication, interactive session channels (`pty-req`, `shell`, `window-change`), and a bounded VT100/ANSI escape sequence parser.
- **Desktop notifications, window edge snapping, and workspaces contracts.** Added freestanding `modus:contract/desktop_layout` implementing non-focus-stealing reserved-corner system notifications with per-sender quotas and TTL expiry, floating window snap-to-edge/maximize/minimize with saved-rect restore, and 9 isolated workspaces with per-workspace focus memory.
- **Unicode Latin NFC, grapheme stepping, line breaking, and French/English collation.** Added freestanding `modus:contract/unicode_latin` checking and composing Latin NFC names, stepping by UAX #29 extended grapheme clusters, wrapping lines by UAX #14 opportunities, and sorting French and English words in multi-level reader order.
- **USB HID boot parser, UAX #9 RTL bidi engine, and sandboxed WebAssembly 1.0 interpreter.** Added freestanding contract packages `modus:contract/usb_hid` (boot keyboard rollover/diff and clamped pointer reports), `modus:contract/rtl_bidi` (Arabic/Hebrew UAX #9 visual reordering and RTL interface layout mirroring), and `modus:contract/wasm_runtime` (WebAssembly 1.0 binary validator and bounded fuel/memory/capability interpreter).
- **The boot program named in the processors of a failed step.** The boot program logs its UEFI loaded image base address and size, and the tool parses that range when reporting processors on a failed step so an instruction inside the boot program names the boot program and its offset instead of "no known code".
- **CJK input method, VNC/RDP gateway framing, and document picker / drop-only drag-and-drop contracts.** Added freestanding contract packages `modus:contract/ime_cjk` (Pinyin/Romaji candidate window and UAX #11 double-width cell widths), `modus:contract/vnc_rdp_gateway` (RFC 6143 RFB 3.8 and RDP TPKT/X.224 framing), and `modus:contract/document_picker` (capability grant registry and drop-only inter-window drag-and-drop).
- **Freestanding `libghostty-vt` design and fixed-arena C-ABI allocator bridge.** Recorded the `libghostty-vt` (MIT) integration design in `src/md/features/ghostty_vt.md` (keeping the small Odin `terminal` in the Zig-free bootstrap seed) and added `modus:contract/ghostty_vt` implementing the bounded fixed-arena `Ghostty_Allocator` vtable and validated terminal options.
- **Intrinsic layout, screen video codec, cross-thread profiler, external pager, and driver-domain keeper contracts.** Added freestanding contract packages `modus:contract/layout_measure` (bottom-up intrinsic sizing), `modus:contract/video_codec` (lossless 16x16 tile-delta + LZ4 screen streaming), `modus:contract/processor_profiler` (hardware counter delta/IPC profiler and W^X external pager), and `modus:contract/driver_domain` (resilient class-ring restart keeper and Linux driver-domain frame proxy).
- **Desktop notifications, separable blur, zero-copy large-page buffers, screen reader / magnifier / Braille, CLDR en/fr localization, and complex script shaping.** Added freestanding contracts `notifications`, `blur_filter`, `zero_copy_buffer`, `screen_reader`, `locale_cldr`, and `complex_shaping`.
- **PCIe bridge/multi-root topology, AMD-Vi IOMMU isolation, MCE/NMI containment, encrypted store pages, multi-machine sync/relay, and browser VM runtime contracts.** Added freestanding contracts `pci_topology`, `kernel_fault_isolation`, `store_crypto`, `machine_sync`, and `browser_vm_runtime`.
- **Freestanding `ghostty_vt` and `web_engine` library modules, compositor desktop features, and browser machine OPFS/WebAudio/non-COI support.** Added shared library modules `src/modules/libraries/ghostty_vt` and `src/modules/libraries/web_engine`, wired workspaces/notifications/magnifier into `compositor`, and upgraded `browser_machine.html` with OPFS persistent disk initialization, WebAudio unlock, and automatic COI vs non-COI mode reporting.
- **Full-system Linux/NT guest contracts, rich-toolkit outside-module imports, resilience/TPM contracts, supervision/governance contracts, and C-world reproducible recipes.** Added freestanding contracts `guest_os`, `rich_toolkit`, `serial_ring`, and `console_prompt`, plus reproducible `chibicc` Stage 1/2, SQLite, Lua, and zstd recipes in `tools/modustool/build/c_world_recipes.odin`.
- **The bar at the bottom of the screen**: `compositor.bar_position(2)` or the item "Bar Position Bottom" of the context menu of the desktop moves the whole bar to the bottom: the mark, the buttons `start` and `windows`, the hint of the palette, the clock, the mark of a viewer, the window plates and the tray badges. The start menu, the menu of the windows, the viewer list and the preview of a window plate open up from the bar; a maximized or snapped window, the notifications, the context menu and the trusted dialogs stay off the bar. A bar at the top keeps each place that it had.
- **The services of the declarations count their work.** `plans` exports `diff_count`, `apply_generation` (the newest generation that an apply committed, which a rollback keeps) and `rollback_generation` (the generation that a rollback with no target selects, with no change), `recipes` exports `build_count` and `cache_hits`, and `projects` exports `pin_count`, the pins of the last `pins.lex` that it read whole.
- **The debugger records the debugged process.** `record_start()`, `record_end()`, `replay_status()`, `replay_checkpoint(thread)` and `replay_step_back(points)`, as exports of `debugger` and as console commands, ask the kernel to write the input history of the attached process, show its input points, its bytes and why it ended, keep checkpoints of a held thread, and give the nearest checkpoint and the input points of a step back; each refusal of the kernel, such as a process that ran already, is the error record of the export.
- **The language list in settings.** A principal orders the languages that it reads with `settings.set_languages`, a table of `language_tag.Locale` records, or with the row "languages" of the window of `settings`, and `settings.languages()` gives the list with the canonical tag of each; each tag is read once into a typed record, its case and its deprecated language made canonical (`iw` is `he`), and a damaged list on the page or in the store is refused whole.
- **Live compositor notifications, viewport magnifier zoom, supervision generation tracking, and console `roles()` command.** `compositor` now renders active desktop notification banners in the top-right corner, applies live 1.5x-8x viewport magnification inside `screen_copy`, and exports `notify(title, body)` and `magnifier(zoom_eighths)`; `root` advances role supervision generations when role holders stop; `console` adds the `roles()` built-in table command.
- **A binary of the tool for each machine test part.** In the check of the flake, each test part runs a binary of the tool that holds the core of the tool and the scenario files of that part alone, which `tools/modustool/machine/test_part_files.json` names. A change of the scenario of one test part then runs that part again and takes every other part from the store, so a developer who works on one scenario waits for one part. A binary of one part refuses `test` of another part and the whole test, and `modustool part_cache` now also proves that a line more in the scenario of the part `debugger` changes the derivation of that part only.
- **Every act of the desktop with the keyboard.** Control+Shift+F7 moves the window in front with the arrow keys and Control+Shift+F8 resizes it, by 16 logical pixels or by 1 with Shift: Enter keeps the result, and Escape gives back the old place and size; the palette offers the same as "move window" and "resize window". Control+Shift+F10 gives the bar the focus of the keys: Left and Right reach the heron button, the button of the windows, each window plate and the viewer mark, Down and Up walk the lines of the start menu and of the menu of the windows, Enter acts and Escape returns to the window. The element with the focus in a window, the item of the bar and the window that the arrows hold now show a 2 pixel outline in the strong text outside them, whose contrast a host test computes at 7.3 to 1 or more on each surface of the dark, light and two high contrast sets.
- **The debugger records a process from its first instruction, with its shared writers.** `record("clock")` starts a module held before its first instruction through the new root command `Debug_Start`, records it, then lets it run. Memory that the process and another process both map with Write is now recorded: the kernel lets that writer run only while the recorded thread is at an input point, at most one stint at a time, and writes what it changed into the history. `replay_start()` runs the module again from the history with the context of the record, and `replay_status()` shows the replay and the input point of a divergence, also after the debugging ended.
- **A restart window for a start row.** A start row can name a restart window in seconds: its count of restarts then holds inside each window, so a holder with a rare fault keeps its role for a run of months, and a holder that stops in a loop ends with one line. A row with no window keeps its count for the whole run and reads no clock. A needed row past its bound after the machine is usable now stops the default start policy with `Failed`, with one line that names the row, its count and its window. The start list is now the version 3, and its border refuses a window on a row with no restart.
- **Per-view independent zoom in `scale_factor`.** Added `View_Zoom` (`0.5x` to `4.0x` in eighths), `view_zoom_step`, `view_screen_pixel`, and `view_logical_pixel` to compose per-view zoom with the desktop scale factor in one exact rounding step, and recorded the design in `src/md/features/desktop_to_browser.md`.
- **The screen through the virtual GPU.** The compositor attaches its framebuffer read-only to `gpu_device` over the VirtIO-GPU 1.2 PCI control queue, binds the 1920 × 1080 2D scanout, and presents damaged rectangles to the host virtual GPU (`compositor.gpu_info()` reports live scanout and present counts).
- **Exports of plans, recipes, projects, the guest builder and the collector**: a caller that acts for its principal now calls `plans.difference`, `plans.apply`, `plans.rollback` and `plans.generations` on its own plans, with a stale or unconsented apply and a rollback with no earlier generation refused by an error record; `recipes.register`, `recipes.build`, `recipes.cache_lookup`, `recipes.builds` and `recipes.realisations`, where a second build of a recipe comes from the cache and a recipe of the inputs tree builder builds in the service; `projects.load`, `projects.update`, which gives the one changed line of `pins.lex`, and `projects.pins`; `guest_builder.status` and `guest_builder.disks_check`; and the bounded spaces of the collector (`space_open`, `record_append`, `records_read`, `spaces`, `space_verify`, `source_count`, `record_count`), where a full audit space or a full forward queue refuses a record in place of a loss and a source never reads its own space.
- **The layout tree of `modus:layout`**: a window can describe its elements as layout boxes with sizes in pixels, a measured text, a percent of an ancestor or the sum of the children, and one solver gives each box its rectangle in whole logical pixels, with the room shared by strictness, alignment, clipping and scrolling; the package also keeps the state of each box across frames, gives the input of a box in one frame, and gives the colors and the spacing of an element by its role from the one table of the color tokens, which host tests check for contrast in the dark, light and high contrast appearances.
- **A font for the missing Greek letters, Cyrillic and Latin Extended-A.** Noto Sans, as the subset "Noto Sans LGC" (87 172 bytes), is the last font of each fallback chain: every window and every cell draws the 49 Greek letters (Π and ρ too), the 96 basic Cyrillic letters (U+0400 to U+045F) and the 128 letters of Latin Extended-A (U+0100 to U+017F), and the compositor names no Greek or Cyrillic letter as missing on its log. A bold face draws these letters in the regular weight of Noto Sans.
- **The assembler takes floating point, atomics and tables of addresses**: `assemble` now takes the scalar SSE2 instructions (`movss`, `movsd`, `movq`, `addsd`, `ucomisd`, `cvtsi2sd`, `cvttsd2si` and the others) on `%xmm0` to `%xmm15`, `xchg` and `lock cmpxchg`, numeric local labels (`1:`, `1f`, `1b`), and `quad label + offset` in the data, which the module rebases at its load.
  So `compile` turns C with `float`, `double`, `alloca`, the atomic builtins and global tables of functions or of strings into a module that runs; a floating constant and `long double` are still refused.
- **The C compiler takes floating constants**: `compile` now reads decimal, scientific and hexadecimal floating constants (`3.141592653589793`, `.5`, `2.5E+10`, `0x1.8p+1`, `1.5f`) and gives each the nearest `double` or `float`, so a C source with a constant of floating point is no longer refused.
- **Built-in per-view zoom in `modus:interface` and live delegated service supervision in `root` and `console`.** `frame_begin` in `modus:interface` now handles independent per-view `Ctrl++`, `Ctrl+-`, and `Ctrl+0` zoom automatically; `root` tracks and supervises all 8 delegated services (`disk_processes`, `principals`, `devices`, `settings`, `powerbox`, `plans`, `recipes`, `projects`) on stop; and `console` adds the `delegated_services()` table command.
- **The console reads a language tag into its record**: `language("fr-CA")` gives the `Locale` record of a BCP 47 tag in its canonical form, which `settings.set_languages([language("fr-CA"), language("en")])` takes, and `language()` gives the first language of the principal. A refused tag names the byte of the subtag at fault and the reason.
- **The console shows the work of services for each connection**: `work("console")` gives one row for each connection of the console to a service, with the state of its work account. No service charges a work budget yet, so each row says `unaccounted`, and another process than the console is refused with the reason.
- **The requested grants in the menu of the windows.** The menu of the windows lists, under each window, each grant that its process asked for and that the principal allowed, with a button Revoke: a press on it, or Enter on the row, ends the grant at once with no console. `compositor.window_grants()` lists the same grants and `compositor.revoke_grant(number)` ends one. `compositor.plan_diff_ask(name, added, removed)` shows the difference of a plan on the trusted display, which the principal accepts or refuses whole.
- **Triangles of the WebGPU subset four pixels at a time**: on the processor, the back end of the WebGPU subset computes the edge functions, the coverage with the top-left rule, the depth and the colors corrected for perspective of four adjacent pixels in one SSE2 register, with the same color bytes and depth values as the scalar form. The host tests compare the two forms on a cube in perspective, translucent triangles, shared edges, scissor rectangles and degenerate triangles.
- **TPM pins and signed-policy commands, ahead of their indices**: the TPM attestation contract now holds the payloads that pin the form of the record of the machine key and the current file of each named principal, refusing a downgrade from a sealed form to `MACHINEK`, an older epoch or generation, a zero or mismatched digest and a damaged payload, and the exact commands `TPM2_PolicyAuthorize` and `TPM2_HierarchyChangeAuth` with a strict read of their answers. The TPM service has the steps that use them; no command runs them yet.
- **Typed objects at the border of the storage service**: the storage service now holds the border of a typed object for the owner of a store: it copies the object, checks its descriptions, its records and the description hash that the owner expects, and runs a derived forward migration or the checked backward migration of a rollback, which refuses the whole object (no clamp, no cut text, no byte written) when a value does not fit. Host tests run one stored type through three releases and back. No module declares a stored type yet, so no store migrates.
- **Byte layouts of the other formats.** The guides show the record of a ring, the error record, the context, the log record, the fault ring and each block of the data disk byte by byte, from the offsets of the compiler. A field of more than 8 rows, such as the 480 rows of the stores of the root block, is drawn folded, with the offsets of its first and its last row.
- **The principals service holds a mirror of the principals**: the root service starts `principals` before the start policy, within three starts, and keeps it told of each named principal, each lock and unlock, the machine passphrase, the whole set of rows of each disk grant table and each allow or revocation of a client key, with no wait in its loop. A `principals` that stops is started again and gets the whole state again; a refusal is one line and changes no grant.
- **The assistive face of the compositor**: `compositor.assistive_attach(nickname, kind)` records a screen reader, a magnifier or a switch input that reads the semantic trees, and the bar shows a mark in the warning color with the word `assistive` and the nickname for each holder while it reads; several holders read at once, and `assistive_detach`, `assistive_holders`, `assistive_magnify` and `assistive_magnify_end` complete the face. A conformance suite of host tests checks that hostile tables are refused with no fault, that an action that the element did not declare never runs, that a protected field publishes nothing of its value, the order of the utterances of a reader, and that a magnifier keeps the box of the focus in its view.
- **A light browser window**: `start("browser")` opens a resizable window with back, forward, an address field and the document origin of the page; the pages built into it (modus://welcome, modus://properties, modus://guide, modus://forms) are HTML and CSS that the contract html_css parses, styles, lays out and paints into the draw table, with links, a history of 32 pages, a form whose values come back in the query, and a refused read of the state of another document origin. The window has no grant but its window: an address of HTTP or HTTPS shows that it has no network.
- **Console record-stream short forms (`keep`, `audit`, `merge`) and live root role connection accounting.** Added `keep(stream)`, `audit(service)`, and `merge(a, b)` wiring list short-form commands in `console`; `root` now resolves role names on `Connect`/`Disconnect` through `role_target_resolve` and tracks per-connection work budgets, supervision generations, and service time attribution via `connection_accounts.odin`.
- **The debugger finds the last input that wrote some bytes.** `watch_back(address, size)` in the console, and `debugger.watch_back(address, size, before_point)`, read the input history of the caller and give the input point whose point copy, shared page or kernel copy last wrote a byte of the range, with the values written, the instruction of the thread there, and the checkpoint before it with the input points of a replay to that write. A damaged history is refused as a whole, and a range with no such write is the error `Absent`, `Recorded_Writes`. A write by an instruction of the process itself is not an input, so it is not in the history.
- **Guest processors, long mode and injected events.** `Guest_Run` takes the index of one of four guest processors of a guest, so that several threads run one guest at once. Its record carries `cr0`, `cr3`, `cr4` and `efer`, a load of real, protected or long mode, and an event that the kernel delivers at the entry. `Memory_Map` maps guest memory at the guest physical address 0 (`At_Address_Zero`), and guest memory made with `Write`, `Execute` and `No_Access` switches between `Write` and `Execute` with `Memory_Protect` and the process handle. The count of kernel operations does not change.
- **An HTML parser that fuzz targets try to break.** The fuzz target `html_css` gives any page of up to 16 KiB to the parser, the style sheet and the layout of the light browser core, and checks the bounds and the links of the tree and of the boxes, and that the border of the drawing takes each draw record of the paint. The fuzz target `software_machine` runs any x86-64 code on the software processor of the browser machine, and checks that a fault changes no register and no byte, and that no step writes a page that the user cannot write.
- **The wire borders of TLS and QUIC in the module `tls_tcp`.** The module now reads and writes the header of a TLS 1.3 record, reads and writes the variable-length integers of QUIC, and reads the form and the version of a QUIC packet header, over bytes and tables of the caller. Each refusal gives an error record and writes no byte: an unknown content type, a legacy version other than 0x0303, a record longer than 16 640 bytes, a negative integer or one over 2^62 - 1, and a short buffer.
- **Unforgeable trusted-display Powerbox grant dialog and Wide-Grant bar mark in `compositor`.** Added `powerbox_dialog.odin` rendering a modal trusted grant dialog (keyboard `Enter`/`Escape`) above all windows, an unforgeable `AGENT GRANT` indicator badge in the system bar with instant `Ctrl+Shift+Escape` revocation, and principal-gated `compositor.powerbox_ask` and `compositor.wide_grant_indicator` exports.
- **Tray widgets on the bar**: a button of the control center and a bell left of the clock, and the clock itself, open a card from the bar. The control center moves the bar and turns the magnifier on or off with a press on a tile, says the appearance and the display in force, and starts `settings`, `monitor`, `browser` and `edit`. The bell lists the last 6 notifications with `Clear all`, and a coral square on it says that the list is not empty. The clock opens the month of today, with the time zone and the time since the boot. One card shows at a time, Escape or a press outside closes it, the keys of the bar reach each part, and `compositor.tray_widget_open(widget)` opens one from the console.
- **The audit spaces of the collector, served to the principal only.** The collector refuses each view of a space to its source: the source cannot read its records, read its state with the new `space_stats`, find it in `spaces` or set its terms. `records_read` takes a limit of 1 to 64 records after a serial, and the new `act_records_read` gives the act records of a wide grant by grant and by a range of sequences. The counts and the chain check of the act records now refuse a caller that does not act for the principal.
- **The record and the replay of one thread at its kernel entries.** A replay now also checks how its process stops: a process that stops where its input history goes on, or a history that ended for another reason than the stop of the process, is a divergence at that input point. Each divergence names its exact input point, also at a damaged or missing record, and `replay_status()` shows what differed, the value of the history, the value of the replay, and the instruction address of the record there. The machine test of the debugger records a process and replays it to the same end.
- **Shared writers of a recorded process.** A process that shares a page with Write with another process is recorded and replayed: the input history names each such mapping, and a replay maps private pages at the same address, so it needs no peer and reads, at each input point, the bytes that the peer wrote during the record. The machine test of the debugger records a process while another one writes their shared page each round, replays the history with no writer to the same end, and replays a copy with one changed byte to its first divergence.
- **chibicc builds itself to the same bytes.** `modustool c-self-host` compiles the 14 C sources of the C compiler with `chibicc` built by clang, makes a module of their texts with the assembler of modus, runs that module on the host to compile the same sources again, and checks that the two modules have one BLAKE3 hash (354 972 bytes, one run). The assembler takes a program of several sources as one text, with the new directives `unit`, `global` and `align`, and the instruction `ud2`; the output of `compile` now starts with `unit`, gives `global` lines, and treats a `long double` as a `double`.
- **A Linux machine for the guest builder.** `Guest_Registers` carries the 64-bit system state of a guest processor (`star`, `lstar`, `sfmask`, `fs_base`, `gs_base`, `kernel_gs_base`; 240 bytes), and `Guest_Run` refuses an address that is not canonical there. The new process-side machine of `modus:contract/guest_os` loads a bzImage or a vmlinux by the 64-bit boot protocol of Linux and gives it a serial port, a timer, a read-only virtio disk of inputs, a writable virtio disk of outputs and a virtio console, with no network. The `build` export of `guest_builder` runs it and reports the BLAKE3 hash of the output disk. Checked by host tests only.
- **Window minimize/maximize/resize grip, CSD borderless mode, urgency-badged notifications, positionable taskbar/tray contract, and filterable fuzzy context menu.** Added minimize/maximize/close title-bar buttons, bottom-right resize triangle grip, optional client-side decorations (`compositor.set_decoration`), urgency icons and balanced padding on notifications, `modus:contract/desktop_shell` (Top/Bottom/Left/Right bar, window grouping, system tray), and the reusable filterable fuzzy right-click menu `interface.Context_Menu_State`.
- **Vulkan commands in the Venus format of Mesa, and a Venus context on the virtual GPU.** `venus_vulkan` now writes and reads the real Venus wire of Mesa and virglrenderer (the instance, the device and its queue, buffers, memory, command pools and buffers, copies, clears, render passes, draws, submits and the Venus ring), and the GPU device service creates a Venus context with its ring blob on a device that carries Venus, takes client streams with `Venus_Submit`, and keeps its 2D scanout unchanged on a device that does not. The VirtIO-GPU contract now has the command words of the Linux header: the blob commands are `0x010C` and `0x010D`, map and unmap are `0x0208` and `0x0209`, and the `SUBMIT_3D` header is 32 bytes.
- **The kernel compiles for WebAssembly.** The kernel builds for `freestanding_wasm64p32` from the same source as for x86-64: an architecture layer gives the instructions, the entries and the return to a process on the software processor, and its only imports are the procedures of the page. It is the base of a browser machine; it does not run modus in a page yet.
- **Machines talk over the streams of remote**: `remote` now answers the frames of paired machines on its QUIC streams, beside the calls of the host, chosen by the first word of each stream. A machine pairs by a one-use code that ends after ten minutes (a wrong code ends the pairing on both machines); a paired machine then reaches only the uses that the plan of the principal gives it. It fetches an object by its BLAKE3 hash and keeps it only when its bytes hash to it, presents a signed grant of another principal (one day at most, or a revocation list), exchanges a store with a three-way merge that keeps both writes of an object as a conflict value until the principal resolves it once (a lost reply and a write during the exchange lose nothing), calls a role held on the other machine (a broken link or a faulty holder gives Unknown_Outcome, never a false refusal), and deposits sealed datagrams on a relay machine for the one receiver of its route, which sees only a routing token and the sealed bytes.
- **Plans give the roles of the machine.** When a principal accepts a plan of principal 0 on the trusted display, `plans` gives the root service the whole set of its role holders with the root ring command `Plan_Roles` before it commits, and the root service takes the set whole or refuses it whole; `plans.rollback()` gives back the holders of the generation that it selects. `plans`, `recipes` and `projects` now start with their own private store, and `plans` with Connect to the trusted display and the start grant `Plans`.
- **The kernel for WebAssembly copies process memory and keeps time**: on a browser machine, the copy of the kernel with the memory of a process walks the page table of the process with the checks of a user access (present, user, write) on each page before the first byte moves, and a controller in software gives each processor its interrupts and a timer on the clock of the page. The kernel for WebAssembly exports only `entry`, `modus_processor_loop_run` and its memory, and in Node a boot record with a memory map starts it to the start of the root service.
- **SQLite, Lua and zstd as modules over the thin libc**: the real SQLite 3.54.1, Lua 5.4.7 and zstd 1.5.7, fetched at the hashes of their recipes, now build into modules with no import and no instruction that reaches the host (1 067 905, 233 497 and 602 137 bytes), and a host test runs SQL with a rollback and `PRAGMA integrity_check`, Lua scripts with coroutines, metamethods and `pcall`, and zstd frames that the zstd of the host reads. The thin libc gains `exp`, `log`, `log2`, `log10`, `pow` and the circular functions, each within one unit in the last place of the C library of the host (two for `tan`).
- **Multi-unit C compilation and program-tier assembly in system modules.** `c_compiler.compile_unit` compiles named translation units with caller-supplied headers and export lists into an on-demand arena, and `assemble` / `assemble.assemble_program` dynamically scale up to the program tier (512 KiB code, 16,384 labels, 4 MiB text) and return the BLAKE3 hash of the built module.
- **The kernel of a browser machine starts its root service from a page**: `modustool browser-machine` builds the kernel for WebAssembly, refuses a file whose imports and exports are not the border of the page, and writes a page that runs it in a worker, with the serial port, the PS/2 keyboard and mouse, the clock and a canvas of 1280 × 800. `modustool browser-machine boot` starts the same host in Node 24 and prints the serial port: the kernel reaches `kernel: ok` and runs the first thread of the root service on the software processor.
- **The audit spaces of the collector in its store.** The collector writes each record of an audit space into its private store, as audit segments of 16 records chained by their BLAKE3 hash, before it keeps or forwards that record; a record that the store refuses is neither kept nor forwarded. A start of the collector reads each audit space back, checks each audit segment and the chain between them, goes on with the next serial, and refuses each step with `Store_Damaged` when one byte of the store changed.
- **The light browser parses HTML after the WHATWG and keeps each origin apart**: the parser follows the insertion modes of the WHATWG with the adoption agency algorithm, tables with foster parenting, comments, doctypes and every numeric character reference, in fixed pools; the CSS engine reads descendant and child selectors with the specificity of the standard, borders, text-align and table layout; an address of HTTP or HTTPS shows the request that the worker of its origin writes; and the storage, the cookies, the form values and the cached style sheet of each origin cross a border of messages that refuses every request for the state of another origin.
- **A recorded process maps memory that it receives after its start**: a process that is recorded can now map a ring or a window surface that another process gives it, in place of a refusal. The input history holds the mapping right after its `Memory_Map`, with its first bytes and each later change by the peer, and a replay maps private pages at the same address, so the console and a window process can be recorded and replayed alone. A mapping that a replay could not hold (more than 96 stand-in mappings or 96 pages, a second mapping of the same object, `Execute`) gives its `Memory_Map` a status.
- **A frozen time stamp counter in a guest.** `rdtsc` and `rdtscp` of a guest exit to its process as an exit `Other` (codes 0x6E and 0x87) with the next instruction, and the machine of a Linux guest answers them from its virtual clock, so a build in a guest reads no time of the machine and no processor number.
- **The interrupt shadow of a guest in its record.** `Guest_Registers` carries `interrupt_shadow` (0 or 1) in and out, and `Guest_Run` refuses an external interrupt or the non-maskable interrupt to inject while it is 1; the machine of a Linux guest waits for the end of the shadow, so no interrupt of a modelled device comes one instruction early after `sti` or `mov ss`.
- **The record output `log` and `log.follow` by name in the console.** `log.follow |> where(level = Warning)` draws the warnings of the whole log as they come and never shows the rows of the console itself, so a follow cannot feed on its own lines; `first(n)` and Escape end it. `wire(server.log, reader.input)` names the log of the module that `start` starts, a typed name whose owner has more than 31 bytes is refused, and `start` refuses a list row that repeats an earlier row, whose reader then takes each record twice.
- **The pairs of the collector, and its store**: a wiring list gives `collector.forward` one ring for each source that asks to forward, named by the new field `paired_row` of its row, and the collector keeps each record of a source in its audit space, in its private store, before the ring of its pair receives it; a full space or a full ring holds the writer, and no record is lost. A pair has the pace `Always_Holds_Writer`, and a consumer that does not read, or a pair that never comes, holds its own source only, never the forward of the other sources. The collector starts on demand with the grants `Serve` and `Store`, so its audit spaces live in a store on the machine.
- **A browser machine takes keys.** A process with ports runs `in` and `out` on the software processor, checked against the port map of its processor, and the PS/2 controller of the page raises line 1 for the keyboard and line 12 for the mouse. So the input service runs, and `modustool browser-machine boot` types `help()` on the PS/2 keyboard once the machine is usable and requires the answer of the console in Node.
- **Plans/Recipes/Projects/Display role conformance suite, trusted Powerbox Store Picker, and delegated stop notice ring.** Added canonical role contracts and conformance tests for `Plans`, `Recipes`, `Projects`, `Display`, and `Powerbox` in `modus:contract/role_contract`, wired the trusted Store Picker dialog (`compositor.powerbox_pick_store`) and `compositor.bar_position` export into `compositor`, and added bounded stop-notice recording in `root/delegated_services.odin`.
- **The plan of principal 0 at the start.** The root service starts `plans` before the start policy and waits, within the hang bound, until it gives the roles of the current plan of principal 0, so the role holders that principal 0 applied hold from the first start of the machine. A machine with no data disk and a rescue start with no wait; a stored plan that a newer boot image cannot hold, or a damaged store, keeps the role holders of the image with one line, and the next `plans.apply` is no longer refused.
- **The outbound link and the distributed builder**: `remote` opens a QUIC link to a paired machine that pins its identity key, and sends it requests for objects, role calls, store offers and the steps of a pairing, each of which ends Answered, Refused, Unknown_Outcome or Unreachable; on that link, a graph of up to 32 recipes is built across paired builders and caches, each realisation checked by its signer and each output byte by its hash, a lost builder replaced by the next one or by this machine, and the mode Verify refuses two output hashes with Determinism_Mismatch. Checked by host tests only.
- **Mesa Venus Vulkan pipeline objects, barriers, and draw-table translation.** `modus:contract/venus_vulkan` now encodes and decodes `vkCreateImage`, `vkBindImageMemory`, `vkCreateImageView`, `vkCreateRenderPass`, `vkCreateFramebuffer`, `vkCreateShaderModule`, `vkCreatePipelineLayout`, `vkCreateGraphicsPipelines`, `vkCmdPipelineBarrier`, `vkCmdCopyImageToBuffer`, `vkCmdUpdateBuffer`, `vkCmdPushConstants` and `vkCmdSetViewport`, with the replies of the new create commands. A 2D draw table becomes, through the WebGPU subset, the Venus stream of one frame: the upload of its vertices, the layout transitions of the image, the render pass with a scissor for each clip, and the readback of the image. The drawing module gives this stream with the new export `draw_table_to_venus_20`; the draw into pixels does not change.
- **The Linux boot of the guest builder, checked on a real kernel and on a program of 64 bits**: the guest builder places an initial archive beside the kernel, starts Linux with `nokaslr` and `nolapic`, decodes `movsx` on a device, and answers `wbinvd` and the registers `PAT`, `MTRRCAP`, `MTRRdefType`, `MISC_ENABLE` and the time stamp counter with no fault. Host tests load the bzImage of Nixpkgs (Linux 6.18, protocol 2.15) by the boot protocol, and run a whole build of a program of 64 bits through the loop of the builder, from its read-only input disk to the tree hash of its output disk; a write to the input disk refuses the build.
- **The console without a desktop, live.** A boot image with no compositor now runs the console in a renderer that draws no window: serial_terminal on the second serial port (COM2, so the log of the kernel on COM1 never mixes with it), or grid_terminal on the screen, with the keys of the PS/2 keyboard. The root service chooses it from the modules of the image and the screen, and a desktop image runs as before. `modustool browser-machine console` writes a console page and its signed console image, with no compositor, drawing module or font; the host of the browser machine plays COM2.
- **An initial archive in the spec of a guest build.** The spec of a guest build names a cpio initial archive by its BLAKE3 hash and its size, and the export `build` of `guest_builder` takes its bytes: the builder copies them to their place in the memory of the guest, checks the hash on that copy before the boot, and refuses a tampered, cut or oversized archive before the guest runs. A build can now run the `init` of its recipe with a kernel of Nixpkgs that carries none. The wire of the spec is version 2, 3240 bytes.
- **The exchange of a Venus command and its reply through the ring.** `venus_vulkan` sends one command with the reply flag and its seek of the reply stream in one ring write, and reads the reply once the head of the renderer passes it, with `venus_ring_submit_command` and `venus_ring_poll_reply`. It refuses a full reply stream, a fatal ring and a reply that it already read, and the decoder of the physical device properties refuses a name that is not UTF-8. Host tests only: no process maps the memory of the device yet.
- **Live VirtIO-GPU 1.2 PCI discovery and on-demand export serving for `plans`, `recipes`, `projects`, `collector`, and `powerbox`.** `root` now scans PCI bus 0 for VirtIO-GPU (`0x1AF4:0x1050`) and starts `gpu_device` automatically at boot, and starts `plans`, `recipes`, `projects`, `collector`, and `powerbox` on demand with live `system.service_open` export rings callable directly from `console` and `remote`.
- **Live VirtIO-GPU scanout bridge in `compositor` and one-step `compile_c` command in `console`.** `root` now hands the VirtIO-GPU client ring to `compositor` (`GRANT_GPU_RING`), which probes `Display_Info` and `Probe_Venus`, attaches its framebuffer, flushes `Present_Rect`, and exposes `compositor.gpu_info()`; `console` adds `compile_c(name, source)` chaining `c_compiler` (`chibicc`), `assemble`, and `isolate_memory` in one Lex call.
- **Pairing state in the remote service**: `remote` now holds the state of pairing by a one-use code (CPace over Ristretto255, bound to the connection and to both machine identity keys), a bounded table of paired machines, the newest signed machine list with removal of each machine that a newer list drops, the check of signed grants (one day at most without a revocation list), and a store that keeps an object from a paired machine only when its bytes hash to the requested hash.
- **The console page runs its machine in a worker, with the trust of its image, and reads an HTTPS object cache.** `browser_machine_console.html` starts the machine in the worker of the browser machine, so the page keeps its scroll and its keys while the kernel runs, and it gives the root service the hash of the description of `console.image` from `console.image.trust`. With `?cache=<address>`, the host fetches blobs and realisations by hash from that object cache over HTTPS with no relay, and the system checks each byte by its BLAKE3 hash and each realisation by its Ed25519 signer; the device through which a process asks for an object comes later.
- **The console page drawn with the document of the browser.** The console image of the browser machine now runs dom_terminal, which writes one JSON line for each change of the terminal on the second serial port, and the console page draws it with plain elements: output lines that the browser selects, copies and wraps, the edit line with its colors of code and a caret, the hint of the call with its parameter marked, a status bar, and a completion list that a click chooses. A dark and a light theme color the whole output again at once. A paste types its text. The page needs no canvas and no library. serial_terminal on the serial port and grid_terminal on the screen stay as they are.

### Changed

- **The root service writes log facts**: each start and each stop of a process, a connection made or ended (for a client key of `remote` too), a grant revoked or given to debug, and the start of a device service are records of fields, whose sentences are those of before. In the console, `log.records(0) |> facts(root.Process_Stopped) |> where(outcome = Done)` gives the clean stops of the processes, with the stop cause and the stop outcome as columns, and the window of the log writes each sentence. The two new contract enums `Stop_Cause` and `Stop_Outcome` name the members in a condition; a sentence writes the words of a stop, "itself, done".
- **The compositor and the windows write log facts**: a surface made, a window shown, in front or closed, the events that a window lost, a refused word of a ring and the element lines of each window and of the palette are records of fields, whose sentences are those of before. In the console, `log.records(0) |> facts(compositor.Window_In_Front) |> where(window = "console")` gives each time that the console came to the front, and a machine test presses an element from the fields of its last element line. The module file of a process lists only the fact types that its code writes.
- **The storage service and the block service write log facts**: a disk that does not answer, the read back of a commit and its root, a check of a commit that fails, the open of the disk with its damaged blocks, a share, a store at its limit, the erase of versions, the stop of the removal of a tree and the commit of range writes are records of fields, whose sentences are those of before. In the console, `log.records(0) |> facts(storage_service.Disk_Opened) |> where(wrong > 0)` gives each open of the disk that found blocks with a wrong check.
- **The device services, the network service, remote and remote_display write log facts**: a device found, a device that does not answer, a refused request, record, word or extend, the bytes and records that the input service lost, an endpoint made and ended, a client key or a viewer key that a handshake refuses or takes, a stream that a bound resets, and a read of the entropy device that the root service counts as refused are records of fields, whose sentences are those of before. In the console, `log.records(0) |> facts(remote_service.Client_Key_Refused)` gives each client key that no principal allowed, and `log.records(0) |> facts(network_service.Endpoint_Ended) |> where(owner = "server")` the end of each endpoint of `server`.
- **The copy of the cryptography of Odin has its own package names.** Each package of `support/cryptography` declares the name of its origin after `modus_` (`package modus_sha2`, `package modus__subtle`), so a host program imports both the copy and `core:crypto` in one build. The imports and the calls do not change: the code calls a package by the last element of its import path. `modustool cryptography-origin` checks the new package line of each taken file as a listed change.
- **A shorter serial line for a log fact.** The serial port writes a fact as `#<module tag>:<key tag> <values>`: the first 4 bytes of the module hash, the first 3 bytes of the fact key, and each value byte that is not zero as two hexadecimal digits, with each run of zero bytes as one letter from `g` (1 byte) to `z` (20 bytes). On the facts of the logs of one full check, the lines take 79 713 bytes in place of 155 820, and on the machines `quic`, `tcp` and `remote_display` they take 0.94 to 0.98 times the bytes of the same lines as text, in place of 1.9 times. The build refuses two module files with one module tag and two fact types of one module with one key tag.
- **Machine test parts as derivations.** The check of the flake runs each test part in a derivation of its own, on the test inputs of a whole build named by the hash of their bytes, so a change of a document or of a host test runs no machine again and Nix takes each part from the store. `modustool screenshots` prints how many parts Nix took from the store and how many ran, and runs at most one part for each four processors. `modustool part_cache` proves the cache: after a change of an intent file or of a host test, each test part has the same derivation. A change of the sources of the tool or of the system still runs every part. Each part names the files of the build that it reads, and the whole build refuses, before any machine starts, a part that reads a file that its inputs lack. The test of the debug objects against the linked files moved from the part `host_modules` to `modustool check`.
- **The demonstrations from address_space to heron write log facts**: their cases, their measures and their failures are now records of fields with the sentence of before, so `facts(disk.Run_Measured)` or `facts(debugged.Breakpoints_Removed)` in the console gives their values as columns. The measure record and the print of a measure run read the sentence of a measured fact, as they read a text line.
- **The test modules write log facts**: each line of the test modules is a record of fields, but the record of `log_reader` and of its children, whose text the test reads back, the two lines that measure the write of a text, the long text of `probe`, and three lines that only a copy of `visitor` built outside the sources writes. Each case of `hierarchy` and `ranges` is a fact type of its own whose last field is its verdict, so a case that fails now ends with "WRONG" (`hierarchy: a tree cannot move inside itself: WRONG`), and `where(verdict = "WRONG")` keeps the failed cases. A status of the kernel, of the storage service, of the block service and of a call, an error kind, an object kind and a record form that a line wrote as a number are now the names of their members (`debug_target: the second thread did not start: status No_Slot`). `log_reader` also checks a fact of several records through the kernel: a fact of 145 value bytes and one at the bound of a call read back record by record and joined whole, and a child whose log limit refuses whole each fact of more records than it has left.
- **unlock writes log facts**: each line of unlock is a record of fields, whose sentence is the line of before, the lines with no value too. A status of a kernel operation or of the root service is written by the name of its member ("status No_Right", where the line said "status 7"); an erase of versions that the store refused writes the fields of its error record ("kind Too_Long, unit Bytes, ..."), where the line wrote the sentence of the console. In the console, `log.records(0) |> facts(unlock.Passphrase_Wrong_Counted_By_Tpm) |> where(principal = 1)` gives each wrong passphrase of principal 1 that the TPM counted.
- **The console evaluates a line with the evaluator of Lex**: each part of a line is computed once, its calls run in the order of the data as before, and an evaluation stops past a budget of 1048576 steps with the column where it stopped. The remainder of the least integer by `-1` is now 0, where the console refused it as a division that does not fit.
- **The other lines of the root service are log facts.** The root service writes each of its lines as a log fact, the lines with no value too: the budgets and the mappings of a start, the devices, the debugging, the resources, the replacements, the refusals of a start from the disk, the disk grants of a nickname, the disk grant tables, the client keys of remote, the principals, the trust of the boot and the start policy, so a reader filters them by field, as `log.records(0) |> facts(root.Table_Written) |> where(principal = 0)`. A status of the kernel names its member ("status No_Memory", not "status 7"), the refusal of the entropy service, of the network service and of the storage service gives the fields of its error record, the machine parts page writes one line for each part, and a load that the loader refuses gives a line of the start that failed, then a fact of the fields of its stage (`root.Load_Find_Failed` and the others), in place of the sentence of the loader. No line of the root service is text any more.
- **The demonstrations from imports to workers write log facts.** Each of their lines is now a record of fields, so `facts()` and `where()` in the console filter them by field: for example `facts(net.Round_Trip_Measured)`, `facts(kernel_events.Operations_Counted) |> where(verdict = "WRONG")`, `facts(scheduler.Strategy_Shares_Measured)`. The measure record, its page and the checks of a measure run read the sentence of a fact as they read a line. Most sentences stay as before; these changed. A status is written by its name where `locks`, `rights`, `tone` and the call host of `tables` wrote its number, a status of an isolated call of `tables` by its name too, and `tone` and `net` write the kind of an error record by its name. A share of `scheduler`, the times of a call and of a bind of `imports` and the rate of the generator of `randomness` have two decimals (`12.34 %`), in the log and in the window of `randomness`. A refusal of `net`, `notes` and `tables` writes its error record as its seven fields (`kind No_Grant, unit Endpoint, operation 2, ...`). A long line is several lines: each strategy of `scheduler` (its wakes, then its shares), its weight limit and the rounds under it, the counts of the server of `quic_echo`, the costs of the rules of `kernel_events`, the lock waits of `locks` (one line for each lock) and its frame caches (one line for each processor). A child of `kernel_events`, a process of the share policy of `scheduler` and the call host of `tables` that did not start write a fact of the status and of the budgets, then a fact of the stage of the load error, with the names and the hash heads of the modules that it concerns (`facts(tables.Call_Host_Load_Find_Failed)`).
- **The windows of the system write log facts.** The compositor, the console, the terminal, `edit`, `manual`, `settings`, `inspect`, `debugger_window`, `monitor` and `log` write each of their lines as a log fact, and a reader filters them by field, as `log.records(0) |> facts(settings_process.Values_Changed) |> where(font = "spleen")`. Most sentences stay as they were; a line of the console, a debug place, a link of the manual and the text of a field of the palette are text views, whole up to the area of a fact. A status is written by the name of its member ("status Bad_Handle"), an error record by its fields ("kind Too_Long, unit Bytes, operation 3, ..."), the problem of a pointer theme file by the names of its part, its field and its rule, the menu of the windows as one line for each window, the list of the palette as its count and one line for each of its first rows, and the stop that `inspect` shows as one line for the fault and one for each frame of the stack.
- **The facts of the other system modules.** The debugger, the start policy, the module file service, the date service, the call host, the windows `boot` and `pages`, `assemble` and `c_compiler` write their lines as log facts, and so do the lines that the TPM, storage, sound, entropy, frame, block, input and network services, `remote` and `remote_display` still wrote as text: the stop of a service with its failure, the lines with no value, the response codes of the TPM, the public keys and the listen ports, the measures, and one fact for each fault of a debug object. A reader filters them by field, for example `log.records(0) |> facts(debugger.Thread_Stopped) |> where(thread = 0)`. Each sentence is the line of before, but for four forms: a status of the kernel or of a client of the storage service is written by the name of its member (`status No_Memory`, not `status 4`), a refusal of the writer of module files in `assemble` too (`refusal Name_Twice`, not `refusal 3`); a refused request of `remote`, a refusal of the block service and a refusal of the store of `module_files` write the values of their error record (`kind Too_Small, unit Bytes, operation 0, argument 0, value 20, limit 104, found 0`) in place of its sentence; and a load error of the call host is one fact of its stage, with the names, the heads of the hashes and what failed (`host: cannot load the module: the kernel refused to map probe (1a2b3c4d…): status No_Memory`). The path of a request of `module_files` is a text view of its fact, whole up to its 200 bytes. A line of the diagnostic of `c_compiler` longer than 143 bytes is one fact for each piece of 143 bytes. No line of these modules stays text.
- **`columns` takes the names of fields**: `columns(t, slot, name)` in place of `columns(t, "slot name")`. Each name is a field of the record, checked before the line runs when the console knows the record on the left of `|>`, colored as a field and offered by the completion; a text of names is no longer taken.
- **Every line of the demonstrations from address_space to heron is a log fact**, but the sentences of the error records that the process `errors` shows: a line with no value, an address, a time with one decimal and the head of a hash are facts now. Some sentences changed: a refusal of `disk`, `fetch` and `address_space` writes its error record as fields (`kind Out_Of_Range, unit Blocks, operation 3, ...`), a status that a failure wrote as its number writes the name of its member, the times in tenths of a nanosecond and the rates of `fetch` have two decimals, the instruction sets of `extended_state` are three lines, and its parts of a save are one line each.
- **The access records of `server` are a record output, and a slow reader slows or loses with a count**: `server` writes the record of each request into its record output `requests`, which a reader wires, in place of a line of the log with a limit of 100 in each second. In the console, after `connect("server")`, `server.requests |> where(status = 404) |> first(5)` gives the requests of the status 404 from the next one on, and "joined after N records" counts those before. `server` never waits for a reader of the console: a full ring refuses the next records with `Full`, and `stream_counts(server.requests)` now says `held` beside `written`, `discarded` and `refused`: the writes that a full ring held until it had room, which a write that then gave `Full` counts in both. It also says `rings_past_gap`: the readers whose gap reached its largest count, whose ring the writer closed. On a ring with a condition, a gap counts 2^31 − 1 records at most, so a reader always tells a record ahead from one behind. A reader wired with the pace `Always_Holds_Writer` holds the server and loses nothing; one wired with the pace `Never_Holds_Writer` reads a gap record with the exact count of the records that it lost, and the other readers are not touched. The log has no access record of `server` any more.
- **The desktop at the scale factor.** The desktop of the screen of 1920 × 1080 is drawn at 1.25 by default, a logical screen of 1536 × 864, and at 1.375 with `settings.set(scale = .eleven_eighths)` or the new row of the settings window: the windows, the bar, the menus, the palette, the text and the pointer, each edge rounded to the nearest pixel of the screen; a scale from 1.5 to 2 is refused with `Out_Of_Range` until each window fits it. At a change of the scale each window lets its surface go and makes one at the scale that the compositor names, from its own budget; the compositor answers each request, a resize too (a refused one leaves the window at the size that it shows, with a line), never loses an answer, refuses a picture of a surface that it does not hold, and cuts each surface to its window, so no window draws over another between two scales. `Surface_Create` names the logical size and the scale of the surface, and a page that the read refuses keeps the scale in force. The pointer and the places of the compositor are logical pixels; a window that draws by hand shows a picture of its logical size; the text size of a draw record is at most 128 logical pixels (version 19, the exports `damage_of_draw_table_19` and `execute_draw_table_in_rectangles_19`); the window pages are counted at 1.375, and each module with a window states its window, so the least budget stays 2048 pages.
- **A failed machine test part prints its whole log.** The tool prints each line of the log of the machine that failed, with its facts written as sentences, in place of its last 25 raw lines; `nix log` of the part keeps it whole after the check.
- **Enum parameters: one description for the completion, the check and the palette**: an export takes a value of a closed set as an enum, and its module file (version 6) names the members and the getter of the value in use. The console completes the members, `.french`, refuses a wrong one at its place before the call, and the palette shows one row of buttons for each such parameter, with the member in use marked. `settings.set` takes one optional argument for each setting, `settings.set(keyboard_layout = .french)`, each setting has its getter, `settings.font()`, and the time zone has `settings.set_time_zone` and `settings.time_zone`. The command `setting`, `settings.get` and the setting entries of the palette are gone; the rows of the settings appear after `connect("settings")`.
- **Property 23: a role is a contract, and its implementation is replaceable.** The display, the console, the storage and each other role of the system become a typed contract that the root service gives to one process, and no other process depends on the module that fills it. The designs of the roles, of their contracts and of the split of the root service into a core and delegated services are written; nothing of them is built yet.
- **Every input of a process passes through a channel that can be recorded.** Property 24 is a rule of the system: a process learns nothing from outside its own code except through a channel that the kernel can record, so a replay can run one process again alone with the same inputs. The design names the four exceptions (a process that holds a device, the root service, a process that runs a guest, and the few instructions that no fault catches), and the cycle counter and cpuid, which a process reads with no fault today, become channels that the kernel traps while a process is recorded.
- **Take the gold, leave the debt.** Property 25 is a rule of the system: modus takes the proven algorithms, structures, standards and lessons of other systems, leaves their compatibility layers, text formats and ambient rights, and wires each idea where its foundations give it more. Each reuse names its source and its first user, a rewritten algorithm keeps a reference, a fuzz test and a measure, code is reused as it is when a rewrite adds nothing, and an idea that contradicts another property stays out.
- **The check of a text reads sixteen bytes at a time.** `text_valid`, which each border calls on a text from another party, now checks UTF-8 with no control character with the algorithm of Keiser and Lemire, with the same answer for every input. On the host, with the target of a module and a load of 1.7: 5949 MiB/s in place of 341 MiB/s on 1 MiB of French and CJK text, and 17742 MiB/s in place of 480 MiB/s on 1 MiB of ASCII.
- **Two terms renamed: start grant set and stint.** What the root service gives a process at its start is now a start grant set (the table `start_grant_sets`, in `src/modules/system/root/start_grant_sets.odin`), so "role" means only a contract. The time that a thread runs on a processor from its pick until it leaves is now a stint, so "turn" means only the unit of angle: the guides, the log lines of the root service and of `scheduler`, and the view of `scheduler` use the new words.
- **The kernel is named `modulus`.** The project now uses three names across its documentation and glossary: `modus` for the operating system, `modulus` for the kernel, and `lex` for the language and runtime.
- **Delegated wiring service.** Record-stream wiring now runs in a separate `wiring` system service holding only attenuated grants (read-only boot image, `Spend`-only per-asker child wiring budgets, `Give`-only process tokens, and wiring rings) and consuming `Process_Stop_Notice` records from the root core's `stops` output.
